# Calevate — Roadmap, Build Order & Decision Log

Version 1.0. No calendar dates (by choice); order and gates are binding. Guiding rule:
**client #1 live on a partly-manual stack beats a finished platform with zero clients.**

---

## 1. Milestone 0 — Unblock (parallel, non-code; start immediately)

- Decide legal entity (BuiltByThree entity vs new LLP) — blocks DLT. [Risk R-01]
- DLT PE registration (₹5,900) + telemarketer/header registrations once entity exists.
- **Bolna Pilot** (Engine Verification Session, OPERATIONS §2, per D-31) — closes the
  open engine unknowns (BYOK fee, webhook behavior, Telugu, latency), confirms primary
  engine, gets commercials in writing.
- Sarvam account + ₹1,000 credits; a GCP project with Vertex AI enabled in `asia-south1`
  plus a service-account key (D-127 — an AI Studio API key is disqualified, not a
  substitute); Exotel/Vobiz rate card in writing.
- Reserve calevate.tech DNS zones (admin., app., api.), Clerk apps (two realms), DO BLR
  project, R2 bucket, GitHub org + repo skeleton, Terraform state.
- Sales asset #0: the live Telugu clinic demo agent on a real number (can be built on
  the Bolna dashboard alone — no Calevate code required; doubles as pilot Gate 3
  material). This is also the pitch tool.

Gate G0: engine scorecard passed + demo agent taking calls.

## 2. Milestone 1 — Client #1 Live (thin vertical slice)

Build ONLY what one client needs; admin can be CLI + SQL where UI would slow us:
- Repo: monorepo (apps/web, apps/api, apps/voice-runtime, apps/workers, packages/shared).
- DB core: organizations, users/memberships/invitations, agents(+prompt_versions,
  extraction_schemas), calls/transcript_turns/call_extractions, leads, usage_events,
  plans, consent_ledger, audit_log — with RLS from the first migration.
- VoiceEngine adapter (Bolna) + webhook receiver (source-IP-verified, idempotent —
  TRD §5) + reconciliation poller (the guarantee of record, D-31).
- Post-call pipeline: recording copy → redaction → extraction → lead upsert → metering →
  hot-lead notification (email first; WhatsApp next).
- Client app v1: login/invite accept, dashboard (calls, outcomes, sentiment), call detail
  (redacted transcript + recording + summary + extracted fields), Leads table (schema-driven
  columns, status, filters, CSV export, owner-role "Call this lead" with optional per-call
  context note — D-21; same compliance pre-checks as webhook dispatch).
- Admin v1: new-client wizard steps 1–5 minimal + test-call gate; KB upload+approve;
  publish/promote.
- Regression harness v1: the mandatory five scenarios + fixture replay + report.
- Ops: Sentry, LLM tracing, alerts that reach a person, backups, pre-launch checklist run.
  — **OpenTelemetry tracing shipped and it crosses every boundary** (BUILD-LOG §24;
  `apps/api/core/observability.py`): `TracingMiddleware` opens the server span and only
  trusts an inbound `traceparent` where the caller is ours — voice-runtime's receiver is
  reachable from the internet, and a stranger's sampled flag is a lever on our trace
  bill — and the W3C traceparent then rides the ARQ payload (`TRACE_KWARG`), so
  voice-runtime → Redis → worker → adapter → Postgres is ONE trace and "where did the two
  minutes go" is answerable. The pipeline and the webhook fast path carry named stage
  spans; span attributes are an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist, because a denylist on a
  tracing API fails open (hard rule 6). **Sentry shipped**, config-gated, with the
  `scrub_event` redaction hook that is not optional. **Langfuse and PostHog config was
  REMOVED, not wired** (D-49): three settings with no client are worse than none, because
  the next reader assumes traces are being recorded. Per-call token cost and the latency
  breakdown TRD §2 describes remain a stated gap; restoring Langfuse is a vendor decision
  plus a decision-log entry, not a wiring job — and it now inherits a REQUIREMENT rather
  than a hook: hard rule 6 is enforced by `_RedactingSpanExporter`, which every span leaves
  through, so a direct Langfuse SDK client would be a second UNFILTERED path. The
  hand-called `redact_trace_payload` this bullet used to name is deleted (D-61).
  **Alerts now reach a person** (D-49): `alert()` still writes the structured ERROR log
  first and unconditionally, and then delivers by email through the same transport as
  hot-lead notifications, with per-fingerprint repeat suppression (15 min) and a global
  token bucket, off the request path on a daemon thread and touching neither the outbox
  nor Redis. It needs `ALERTS_EMAIL` + an SMTP host to be configured; a deployment with
  neither says so at boot. **Backups: the mechanism ships, unapplied** (D-50) —
  `infra/backup/` carries wal-g continuous archiving with `archive_timeout = 300`, a
  nightly base backup, an independent offsite encrypted dump, a four-check watchdog and
  systemd timers, with every secret a reference. Nothing has been applied and no wal-g
  command has been run, so the pre-launch checklist's "backups verified" is ticked by the
  drill (`runbooks/backup-restore-drill.md`) passing once, not by this tree existing.

- **Inbound receptionist is the headline capability (D-38)** — it is Outpero's confirmed
  unbuilt flank and is consent-clean by construction (the caller initiated), so it also
  de-risks the self-serve motion. Build and demo it first.
- **Schema-for-scale (D-39):** credits/prepaid ledger, `plan_tier` on organizations, and
  invitations ship in M1 even though the self-serve UI does not — metering and tenancy
  are not retrofittable (D-12).

Gate G1: a paying client (pilot pricing allowed) live on inbound; lead-in-2-min SLO met
for 7 consecutive days; zero cross-tenant test failures.

## 3. Milestone 2 — Outbound + Multi-Client

- Campaigns end-to-end with the compliance gate (blocked features visibly explained);
  instant-lead-callback webhook ingest (Meta/website/Sheets); retry/escalation; DNC.
  — **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §30, §31, §32, §41, §47). **Escalation after retry exhaustion
  now shipped too**: a contact whose dial ladder is spent enqueues
  `escalate_campaign_contact` (registered in `WorkerSettings`, once per contact, its own
  retry ladder), which runs through `check_dispatch` itself rather than a
  messaging-shaped copy of the rules, and every non-delivery is recorded on the lead
  timeline — "we never followed up" is a fact the client can see. What it cannot do is
  send: see the WhatsApp bullet below. Meta Lead Ads ingest is now NATIVE
  (`GET|POST /hooks/v1/ingest/meta/{webhook_id}`) rather than "a webhook you could point
  at us", with one honest hole — the Graph read that carries the form answers needs a
  Page token this deployment does not hold (SURFACES §2b).
- WhatsApp follow-up + hot-lead WhatsApp alerts. — **the TRANSPORT is the single thing
  blocking a finished feature, and as of this pass it is the only thing**
  (`apps/workers/whatsapp.py`: the `WhatsAppTransport` protocol, a console dev sink, the
  delivery record and the retry ladder). This bullet used to read "still seam only",
  which was true of the transport and quietly false of everything upstream of it:
  `enqueue_hot_lead_whatsapp` had NO CALLER, so the seam was not merely unfinished, it
  was unreachable — flipping a BSP on would have delivered nothing and looked
  configured. `notify_hot_lead` now calls it on the same detection that sends the email,
  and the email dedupe carries a `channel` predicate so the two do not eat each other's
  delivery record. Deliberately NO vendor adapter: §6 contains no decision choosing a WhatsApp
  Business Solution Provider, and an adapter written against an imagined API looks
  finished. Messages are template-name + locale + positional variables — the module has no
  free-text body field at all, because a business-initiated message needs an approved
  template. What DID land is the consent the send needs: the `messaging` purpose on
  `consent_ledger` (migration `c2f7a91b4e63`) plus
  `POST /v1/compliance/messaging-consent`, because **consent to be CALLED is not consent
  to be MESSAGED** and nothing was backfilled from one to the other. So the escalation
  path is complete end-to-end except for the transport, and a contact who has opted in is
  refused today as `whatsapp_disabled` (the channel stays off until a human finishes the
  WABA/template checklist) and, once it is switched on, as `no_provider_configured`.
  Picking a BSP is a decision-log entry, and it is what unblocks this bullet.
- One-click AI callback on needs-follow-up calls (D-21): re-dispatch the agent with
  prior-call context via the engine's context-injection webhook. — **shipped**
  (BUILD-LOG §35).
- Outbound CRM sync (D-23): HMAC-signed outbound webhooks + Google Sheets sync
  (lead.created/updated, call.completed events; config table shipped in M1).
  — webhook half **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §34); **Google Sheets sync shipped**
  (BUILD-LOG §47) — `POST /v1/integrations/endpoints/sheets`, delivered through the SAME
  job as the webhook half so it reuses the delivery id, the retry ladder and the
  exhaustion alert rather than minting a second definition of "we delivered a lead", with
  column order held in a JSON array (JSONB does not preserve key order) and an event with
  no declared order REFUSED rather than guessed. **The vendor adapter is now built too**
  (`apps/workers/google_sheets.py`, `GOOGLE_SHEETS_PROVIDER=service_account`): a service
  account the client shares their own document with — chosen over an installed-app OAuth
  flow because sharing is per-document, revocable by the client without us, and holds no
  per-tenant refresh token — minting its own RS256/JWT-bearer token and calling
  `spreadsheets.values.append` with `RAW` + `INSERT_ROWS`. No SDK was added; `pyjwt[crypto]`
  and `httpx` were already in the lockfile. Sheets has NO idempotency key, so a retried job
  reads the delivery-id column before writing and reports `already_present` rather than
  duplicating a row, and a probe that cannot read refuses to write at all.
  `sheets_delivery_available()` remains the one selector both the config route and the
  worker ask, and a deployment with no credential still refuses the endpoint at creation
  rather than accepting rows into silence. **Still owed: a live confirmation against a
  real Google project** — nothing in CI has spoken to Google (OPERATIONS §2 gate).
- Billing surfaces: usage panel (client), margin panel (admin), invoice generation,
  Razorpay links, caps UI. — usage + margin panels **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §36),
  invoice generation **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §40); the **caps-editing UI shipped**
  (client-settable `plans.client_cap_min`/`client_cap_spend`, effective cap =
  `LEAST(admin, client)`, `GET`/`PUT /v1/billing/caps`, SURFACES §2b) and the
  **value-tier rate column shipped** (`plans.overage_rate_value`; NULL = bill everything
  at `overage_rate`, which is every existing plan — **the retail number itself is an
  OPEN founder decision**, deliberately not derived from TRD §10.1's unmeasured bands).
  **Server-side order creation shipped** (D-98) and this line said the opposite for a
  wave: `PROVIDER_CREATES_ORDERS` is True, `RazorpayOrders.create_order` exists and
  `POST /v1/orders` is mounted, creating the order server-side so
  `notes.calevate_tenant_id` reaches the provider by construction. The payment CAPABILITY
  is expressible through one `payment_capability()` selector shared by the intent route
  and the receiver, refusing in problem+json and writing nothing. **What is still not
  true is that anyone can pay**: `PaymentCapability.creates_orders` is False on every
  deployment for a different reason than it used to be — `no_api_secret`, carried
  separately from `reason` so "why can I not pay" and "why is there no order" stay
  distinguishable — and no checkout WIDGET was built at all (hard rule 9; the signed
  webhook is the source of truth, never the browser callback). So `provider_order_pending`
  still stays true in practice, on a credential rather than on missing code (SURFACES §2c). Two additions since: every money reader resolves the plan **in effect**
  rather than the newest row (D-46), and ops can re-derive a stuck cap flag —
  `POST /v1/ops/tenants/{tenant_id}/spend-cap/recompute`, which recomputes and never
  writes the flag directly, closing the dead end where a client raised past their ceiling
  stayed stopped until they acted themselves or the IST month rolled over.
- Admin polish: onboarding wizard complete, client health overview, prompt rollback UI.
  — prompt rollback UI **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §33, §40, §41), and the **client health
  overview now genuinely has** — this line previously claimed it on the strength of
  `GET /v1/admin/tenants`, which is the client DIRECTORY (roster + counters) and answers
  "who are my clients", not "which of them is about to churn". Both surfaces carried the
  title; only one carries it now, and the directory's summary was renamed rather than left
  to compete. The board is `GET /v1/admin/client-health` (`org:read`, admin realm,
  `/admin/health`): an EXCEPTION report of live accounts with at least one signal, most
  broken first, and an account with nothing wrong is absent rather than green. FIVE
  signals, each one an operator can act on today — `calls_stopped`, `outbound_blocked`
  (composed from the dial gate's own predicates, so it cannot disagree with the refusal
  the client sees), `spend_cap_near`, `deliveries_failing`, `knowledge_waiting`. Latency
  and answer-rate tiles from SURFACES §1 are deliberately NOT on it: `calls.latency` was
  dropped in `f1a7c39d5be2` and D-49 removed the trace config, so neither can be observed
  honestly and a tile would be a fabricated number. The call trend carries a `basis`
  (`measured` | `too_new` | `no_baseline`) for the same reason `after_hours_basis` exists,
  because "your calls collapsed" said about a four-day-old account is an accusation the
  data does not support. Cross-tenant with NO RLS policy widened: the directory under
  `app.admin`, then each tenant's own session (`admin/health.py` states the measured cost,
  ~6.5ms per account, rather than asserting it is cheap). The **intake step shipped as an
  API** (BUILD-LOG §45;
  `GET|POST /v1/admin/tenants/{tenant_id}/agents/{agent_id}/intake`, FLOWS §1's eight
  fields and no invented ninth, writing the `compiled_t0_context` D-39 had reserved) —
  and **the wizard now calls it**: `/admin/new` is three steps (account → intake →
  invite), with the draft held above the step swap so answers survive walking forward and
  back, a failed prefill GET WITHHOLDING the form rather than showing an empty one to
  retype over, and the eight fields with no invented ninth. Number provisioning and the
  test-call gate stay absent rather than stubbed, because they are pilot-gated and a stub
  for either would be a screen that lies. **One honest hole, stated on the screen**:
  ~~`save_intake_draft` … NO ROUTE EXPOSES IT~~ — **CLOSED, and this paragraph was
  stale for several sessions.** `POST …/intake/draft` exists (`admin/routes.py`), and
  `IntakeStateOut` carries both `language_primary` and the sheet's `agent_id` provenance;
  all three landed in `60944da`. Kept struck through rather than deleted because the entry
  is now evidence of a defect class this repo produces: a doc that describes a hole
  somebody already filled sends the next session to rebuild it. A survey caught this one
  by grepping for the route instead of trusting the sentence.
- Vertical template #2 fully dressed (whichever client #2 is: clinic or real estate).
  — **real estate shipped** (not written up in the BUILD-LOG; the artefacts are the
  evidence): the `VERTICAL_TEMPLATES["real_estate"]`
  extraction schema AND the half that makes it a template rather than a column list —
  its own `re_*` golden transcripts covering the mandatory five, with
  `tests/vertical_real_estate_test.py` failing when schema and fixtures drift apart. A
  field nothing scores is a column no model is ever measured on.

- **Self-serve tier goes live (D-34):** signup + Google OAuth, credit top-up, number
  purchase with KYC gate. Ships WITH its R-11 mitigations — fixed calling hours, DNC on
  every path, mandatory disclosure, consent ledger, spend caps, and manual review of each
  account's first campaign. No self-serve calling before those exist.
  — signup, the top-up intent and the payment receiver **shipped as surfaces, not as a
  checkout** (SURFACES §2c: `self_serve_signup_enabled` defaults OFF, and server-side
  order creation is not built). **KYC shipped** (D-47), and it was the second-to-last
  mitigation with nothing behind it. Of the R-11 list, five hold in code today
  (platform-fixed calling hours in `within_calling_hours`, DNC on every dispatch path, NOT NULL disclosure, the
  consent ledger, admin + client spend caps) and **the sixth now does too**: the
  first-campaign manual-review hold ships as `first_campaign_reviews`, scoped to the
  TENANT so it cannot be skipped by launching a second campaign or deleting the held
  one, enforced in both `launch_blockers` and `dispatch_blockers`, released only through
  the audited ops route. All six R-11 mitigations are therefore in code — which removes
  the build-side objection to turning `self_serve_signup_enabled` on, though the switch
  itself stays a founder call. The discovery half shipped with it:
  `GET /v1/admin/compliance/holds` is the ops work list of accounts waiting on a human,
  covering BOTH gates, so neither mitigation depends any longer on the client emailing to
  say they are stuck.
- Integration DX (SURFACES §2b): webhook activity view, test-webhook, "Needs attention"
  queue, two-speed publishing. — activity view, test-webhook and the attention queue
  **shipped** (BUILD-LOG §37, §38, §39); **two-speed publishing shipped** (BUILD-LOG §47)
  — admin `POST …/apply` (staged version as the CAS token) and `POST …/undo`, the
  per-agent call cap on the same page, and the client-side unsaved-changes banner read
  from `GET /v1/agents/{agent_id}/pending`. Pending is DERIVED from two pointers, never
  stored as a flag that can disagree with them.

Gate G2: 2–3 clients incl. one outbound campaigner; DLT template approved and a
compliant campaign completed; margin per client visible and ≥ target.

## 4. Milestone 3 — Moat Features

- Managed RAG/memory service (D-28) in the call path: in-call retrieval moves off the
  engine's built-in KB onto the provider (custom tool / thin endpoint, whichever the
  bake-off measured faster); T0 compiled-context automation; knowledge-gap report
  driving KB updates. CRM-side provider integration lands earlier (M2, with the
  bake-off).
- Regression suite growth to 50–100 scenarios/client + red-team set; client-facing QA
  report (sales asset). — **both shipped.** 110 golden cases (58 clinic / 52 real estate)
  of which 30 are red-team, across disclosure suppression, direct AND indirect prompt
  injection (OWASP LLM01:2025 — the indirect form is the one that reaches an extraction),
  PII exfiltration, DNC evasion in both directions, extraction poisoning and degenerate
  input. One executable honesty rule governs the set: `test_every_red_team_case_bites_
  without_a_model` requires each case to carry at least one assertion our own code scores
  against the real attack text, because the offline stand-in is not an LLM and a case whose
  only failure mode is "a model was persuaded" cannot be scored today. `must_not_say` stays
  reference-only until OPERATIONS §3's replay-into-call exists, and says so where a run
  enforces it. **QA report**: `make qa-report CLIENT=<slug> VERTICAL=<...>`, Markdown,
  deterministic, headlined by the DEFECT COUNT rather than a pass rate — defects map onto
  `NON_WAIVABLE_KINDS`, the one promise the harness keeps, whereas a raw pass rate against
  the offline model underclaims a client's agent. Aggregation IS the redaction (per-case
  rows would carry titles that paraphrase caller content), and `basis` follows
  `after_hours_basis`: no percentage under 12 scenarios, no trend from one month. The
  month-over-month comparison is a stated seam, not a half-wired field.
- Call-corpus embeddings (learn from resolved calls). — **not built**, and blocked by D-28:
  RAG is a managed API service and no provider is chosen.
- A/B greeting/script testing with conversion attribution. — **shipped for OUTBOUND**
  (BUILD-LOG §54). The variant a call ran is a STORED FACT written in the same transaction
  as the `calls` row, never re-derived — the split is mutable, so a read-time recompute
  silently re-attributes history the moment anyone ramps the weights. Wilson score
  intervals per arm and Newcombe's hybrid interval for the difference, with
  `MIN_CALLS_PER_VARIANT = 40` taken from Fagerland/Lydersen/Laake rather than from taste;
  below it no comparison is published at all, and only a `winner` verdict gets a badge.
  **Inbound is attributed by FACT or not at all** (D-60). `workers/pipeline.py` now
  records the arm when the engine reports that an ARM's own agent ran the call — its own
  ref, so no inference — and records nothing when it reports the agent's. An inbound
  caller reaches the client's number, which answers with the agent (FLOWS §3), so in
  today's provisioning they carry no arm; drawing a bucket for them after the call would
  credit a script they never heard. An inbound-only agent is still refused, for that
  reason rather than for a missing lookup, and `attributed_directions` is now READ from
  the assigned calls instead of being the literal `("outbound",)`. The same lookup closes
  a real outbound gap: a webhook that beat `dispatch_call`'s commit used to leave an
  arm-dialled call carrying no arm at all. Because an arm can now be credited with a call
  nobody dialled, each arm's counts are resolved by DIRECTION (D-62): `outbound_dialled`
  is the calls we placed, and `inbound_attributed` says how much of the rate's
  denominator was never split — a number, so the screen can qualify the rate it renders
  rather than leaving it to the coverage sentence.
- Client staff roles, lead assignment, lead_events timeline. — **all three shipped**
  (BUILD-LOG §53, §54). Assignment closed `Lead.assigned_to`, an `UNWIRED_BASELINE` entry
  since the first migration; the timeline PROJECTS `lead_events.payload` key-by-key rather
  than serialising a schemaless column six producers write. Staff roles complete it: a
  client owner can now invite, re-role and remove, where every membership change used to be
  an operator running SQL. The last owner cannot be removed or demoted, and the guard locks
  the whole owner SET rather than the target row — EvalPlanQual is blind to rows the query
  never selected, and a `count(*) > 1` check lets two concurrent demotions both commit to
  zero owners, which is a tenant recoverable only by hand.

Gate G3: retrieval T3 p95 ≤ 100ms measured; QA report shipped to every client monthly.

## 5. Milestone 4 — Phase-2 Engine (own orchestration) [enter only on trigger]

Trigger (pre-committed in TRD §10): sustained > ~10–15k min/month across tenants OR a
feature blocked by rented pipeline (T1/T2 tiers, custom turn-taking) OR engine risk event.
- Runtime behind the same VoiceEngine interface; Vobiz/Exotel SIP direct; models
  unchanged (the Sarvam in-call stack; Gemini is post-call only and on Vertex AI
  `asia-south1` per D-127, so a self-hosted runtime does not touch it). Two candidates, decided at the gate on measured
  evidence, not now (D-32): **Pipecat on CPU droplets** (D-02's original plan; first-party
  Exotel serializer + Sarvam plugins; we build the per-call dispatcher and scale-out) or
  **LiveKit Cloud** (ap-south Mumbai region verified; first-party Sarvam plugin; turn
  detection + barge-in in-SDK; managed scaling — but concurrency tiers cap at 5/20/600
  with a 10× cost cliff past 20, and its economics only beat the alternatives above
  ~5k min/month). Both carry the same Indic caveat: **turn detection is an orchestration
  property BYOK models do not fix** — LiveKit's detector excludes Telugu and degrades to
  VAD-only; budget for endpointing work (e.g. Sarvam stt-mode) on either path.
- Shadow mode: mirror a % of calls; compare latency/quality/cost vs the rented engine via the
  regression suite + usage_events; migrate tenant-by-tenant.
Phase 3 (GPU self-host) stays parked until >~50k min/month AND open Telugu TTS reaches
Bulbul-class quality — revisit quarterly, decide on usage_events data, not vibes.

## 6. Decision Log (binding; append entries to change anything above)

| # | Decision | Choice | Why (short) |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | Brand/domain | Calevate / calevate.tech (WHYB rejected) | Distinct product brand under BuiltByThree |
| D-02 ⚠SUPERSEDED by D-31 (engine pick only; phase-2/3 legs stand) | Engine strategy | Rent now (ThinnestAI primary, Bolna fallback) → own orchestration phase 2 → GPU phase 3 | Utilization math + adapter portability |
| D-03 | Not OmniDimension | — | Competitor's supplier; no differentiation; USD; prompt-size ceiling |
| D-04 ⚠SUPERSEDED by D-20 then D-36 | Models | Saarika STT · Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite (→3.x Oct '26) · Bulbul v2 (v3/Aero pending bake-off) | Cost/quality/India-residency balance |
| D-05 | Telephony | Vobiz/Exotel; inbound-priced favorably | India rates; DLT maturity |
| D-06 | Architecture | Modular monolith + isolated voice-runtime + workers; NOT microservices | 2-person team; bulkheads+interfaces give the isolation/swappability |
| D-07 ⚠AMENDED: pgvector→D-28 managed RAG service; auth reaffirmed by D-37 | Stack | FastAPI/Python + Next.js/TS; Postgres+pgvector; Redis+ARQ; Clerk; DO BLR | One backend language; voice ecosystem is Python |
| D-08 ⚠SUPERSEDED by D-28 (its latency physics still governs provider REGION) | RAG | In-Postgres pgvector hybrid, 4-tier latency design; Cohere Embed v4 (BGE-M3 later); no external vector DB | Network RTT kills in-call retrieval |
| D-09 | Extraction | Schema-driven, post-call, per-client custom fields + vertical templates | Product-vs-service core |
| D-10 | Tenancy | Flat (no white-label) ; admin/client separate realms; slug-based client URLs | Direct-clients-only decision |
| D-11 ⚠EXTENDED by D-34 (managed tier unchanged; self-serve tier added) | Pricing | Setup ₹40–75k + retainer ₹15–30k incl. minutes + overage ₹6–8/min; never pure per-minute | Fixed-cost math at 1–2 clients; sell vs telecaller |
| D-12 | Billing | Append-only usage ledger with our cost per unit, from day 1 | Pricing deferable, metering not retrofittable |
| D-13 | Hosting region | DO Bangalore despite "no residency constraint" | Latency co-location; DPDP follows the caller anyway |
| D-14 | Compliance posture | Disclosure always; consent ledger; PII redaction; 90-day recording floor; campaign gate | TRAI/DPDP as researched July 2026 |
| D-15 | Quality process | Regression on every change; mandatory-five scenarios; client-facing QA reports | Industry norm + differentiator vs Outpero |
| D-16 ⚠READ 'ThinnestAI'→'Bolna' per D-31; sub-accounts now exist (TRD §5) | Engine account structure | ONE ThinnestAI account for all clients (agents mapped to tenants in OUR db); NOT account-per-client | Their docs show no workspace/sub-account feature; separate accounts fragment BYOK keys/webhooks/billing and likely breach ToS; noisy-neighbor risk solved by our dispatcher's inbound-reserve + per-tenant ceilings (FLOWS §5); confirm ToS + concurrency in verification item 8 |
| D-17 | Python module layout inside `apps/api` | FLAT (`apps/api/engine/`), not `apps/api/src/engine/` | Resolves a v1.0 conflict: the CLAUDE.md hard rule named a `src/` layout while DEV-SETUP §2's run command (`apps.api.main:app`) requires a flat one — both could not hold. Flat keeps the documented uvicorn/arq module paths working with the repo root on `sys.path`, and `apps/*` stay virtual uv workspace members (not installed packages). Hard rule 2 is about the ENGINE ISOLATION BOUNDARY, not the literal path; it is unchanged in force. CLAUDE.md updated to say `apps/api/engine/`. |
| D-18 | `voice-runtime` directory name | Keep the HYPHEN (`apps/voice-runtime`); start it with `--app-dir` | Resolves a v1.0 conflict: the canonical repo layout is hyphenated but DEV-SETUP §2 gave `apps.voice_runtime.main:app`, which Python cannot import (hyphens are illegal in module names). The layout wins because it is stated in CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md and TRD §1, and the deployable's name appears in infra/CI. Run command becomes `uvicorn main:app --port 8100 --app-dir apps/voice-runtime`. DEV-SETUP updated. |

Corollary: `create-next-app` also emits an `apps/web/AGENTS.md` pointing at `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`, which does not exist in 15 — deleted as misleading; delete it again if a re-scaffold recreates it. |
| D-20 ⚠CORRECTED by D-35 (Bulbul v2 is NOT discontinued — live at half price) and extended by D-36 (LLM→Sarvam) | Sarvam model versions (supersedes the model versions in D-04; the D-04 rationale stands) | Saaras V3 STT · Bulbul V3 TTS | ThinnestAI's docs (Jul 2026) list only Saaras V3 (22 languages, streaming, code-mixed) and Bulbul V3 (11 languages); Saarika v2.5 and Bulbul v2 appear discontinued on the platform. TTS cost line moves to the V3 band (TRD §10). Ear-test at verification is now V3 vs bundled voices. |
| D-21 | Client CRM control boundaries (post Outpero teardown, Jul 2026) | Clients (owner role) CAN: dispatch a single AI call from the Leads table with an optional per-call context note (M1); trigger an AI callback on needs-follow-up calls — agent re-dispatched with prior-call context via the engine's context-injection webhook (M2). Clients CANNOT in v1: edit extraction schemas (admin-only) or customize lead statuses (fixed enum). | Competitor parity where it demos well — Outpero ships manual dispatch and a human-callback button; our callback is AI-closed-loop, one better. The managed-service moat (we curate schemas and quality) stays intact: schema edits regenerate prompt hints and need a regression run, so they route through us; analytics and hot-lead rules key off the fixed status enum. Both client-initiated paths run the same compliance pre-checks as webhook dispatch (DNC scrub, calling hours, caps) and meter like any call. BRD §4.7. |
| D-22 | Admin impersonation | READ-ONLY "view as client" from the admin console, every session + page view audit-logged (actor=admin_user, tenant, at, ip). No acting-as: mutations still go through admin surfaces. | Debugging "my dashboard looks wrong" without screenshot ping-pong; read-only keeps the audit trail unambiguous (no dual attribution). Shapes auth from the first migration: impersonation issues a scoped read-only session against the client realm, never a client credential. Industry-standard support pattern. |
| D-23 | Outbound CRM sync (us → client's tools) | Outbound webhooks (HMAC-signed, our standard envelope, retries + delivery log per SEC-COMP §5) + Google Sheets sync, landing in M2 with campaigns. The `outbound_webhooks` config table is designed in M1 alongside `inbound_webhooks` so no migration pain. | Outpero markets CRM integrations (Zoho/Salesforce/Sheets); a visible checkbox in sales conversations. M2 not M1: client #1 gets CSV export first, revenue before plumbing. webhook_deliveries (direction=out) already covers the forensic side. |
| D-24 | Real-time UI transport | v1 (M1): TanStack Query polling (interval + window-focus refetch) — meets the 2-min post-call SLO with zero new infra. Upgrade with M3: SSE event stream pushing invalidation hints ("what changed", never payloads), TanStack Query refetches. WebSockets explicitly NOT planned. | Boring-solutions doctrine. Our flow is strictly server→client (live call tiles, lead toasts); SSE is plain HTTP (no proxy/infra changes), simpler than WebSockets on both ends, and 30–50% cheaper per connection; WebSockets only pay off with client→server streaming, which we don't have. Events-as-hints keeps auth and staleness out of the stream. SURFACES.md §3.2. |
| D-25 ⚠READ 'ThinnestAI'→'Bolna' per D-31 | Site hosting decoupled from voice path (narrows D-13's scope; its latency reasoning stands) | The calevate.tech site stack (web, api, workers, webhook receiver) hosts on a general-purpose VPS (Hetzner-class) — India co-location NOT required for it. The India requirement applies only to future IN-CALL-PATH services: the M3 pgvector RAG tool endpoint (100ms budget) deploys to an India-region host when built; nothing else relocates. | Founder correction (Jul 2026): in v1 ThinnestAI hosts the entire live call — our site never sits in the caller's audio path, so D-13's co-location argument doesn't apply to it. It DOES apply to the M3 in-call tool endpoint (EU→Mumbai RTT ~120–150ms alone blows the 100ms budget), so the requirement is pinned to that service, not the site. DEPLOYMENT.md §0. |
| D-26 | Production database hosting | Host PostgreSQL 16 (+pgvector) on the VPS — raghava-proven pattern; NOT managed Postgres for now. Consequence accepted and closed: nightly dumps alone would break OPERATIONS §5's RPO 15min, so continuous WAL archiving (wal-g → R2) is REQUIRED, plus nightly pg_dump offsite + quarterly restore drills. | Zero marginal cost; the exact pattern already runs in production for raghava-organics (host PG, containers reach it via host.docker.internal). Managed PG (~$15–30/mo, built-in PITR) is the fallback if wal-g operations prove annoying — migration is dump/restore + connection string. DEPLOYMENT.md §7. |
| D-27 | Edge: Cloudflare proxied | All calevate.tech zones proxied (orange), SSL mode Full (strict), origin locked to Cloudflare IP ranges, Cloudflare Origin CA cert on the default_server block (the 525 fix), certbot certonly (never --nginx). PLUS one fix raghava lacks: real_ip restoration (set_real_ip_from CF ranges + real_ip_header CF-Connecting-IP) so rate limits and audit logs see real caller IPs. | DDoS protection + hidden origin at no cost, and the full config with its failure modes (525/redirect-loop/default-block traps) is already documented and running for raghava. hooks.calevate.tech (engine webhooks) stays proxied too — HMAC authenticates the caller and the CF hop is irrelevant to a 5s webhook budget. DEPLOYMENT.md §5. |
| D-28 ⚠READ 'ThinnestAI'→'Bolna' per D-31; refined by D-33 (KB is not a BYOK slot) | RAG/memory = managed third-party API service (supersedes D-08's self-hosted pgvector plan; D-08's latency physics now governs provider-region choice) | One managed RAG/memory service, consumed via API, serving BOTH the ThinnestAI pipeline AND the client CRM (semantic search, caller memory, future CRM features). We run no vector infrastructure. v1 in-call retrieval stays on ThinnestAI's built-in KB (zero network from their pipeline); the provider takes the in-call path only when its measured p95 from GCP Mumbai fits the 100ms budget. Provider TBD via the bake-off gate in TRD §6 (candidates: Qdrant Cloud, Pinecone serverless [Singapore], Turbopuffer, Weaviate Cloud; memory-API class: Mem0, Supermemory, Zep) — scored on Mumbai latency, per-tenant namespace isolation, hybrid search, ingestion API, memory semantics, DPDP deletion-with-proof, cost. Ingestion approval gate and dual-push (engine KB + provider) remain ours. | Founder direction (Jul 2026): fast, comfortable, API-only RAG shared by pipeline and CRM. Ops-light beats infra ownership for a 2-person team, and the CRM-side uses are latency-tolerant today. The in-call gate keeps D-08's physics honest: nearest serverless regions are Singapore-class (~35–70ms RTT from Mumbai) — plausible but unproven; measure before committing the call path. kb_chunks/pgvector tables in DATA-MODEL become contingency, built only if the bake-off fails. |
| D-29 | Executable governance + git workflow | Guardrail pack (fitness functions) in CI: the critical four ship in M1 (import-linter engine-isolation contract; RLS-coverage check against pg_policies; serializer redaction-exposure scan; .env↔Settings parity), the rest incrementally with the milestone that creates the surface (ledger-immutability, coverage ratchet, docs-drift, openapi-client freshness, compliance invariants, webhook-storm stress, error-budget release gate). Git: trunk-based, short-lived branches, Conventional Commits enforced by pre-commit hooks (ruff + commit-msg lint, <2s). ENGINEERING-PRACTICES.md is the canonical spec. | Raghava-organics runs ~20 such checks in production with a tiny team — proof the style works; industry consensus (fitness functions run with tests, boundaries enforced in CI, coverage ratchets not targets). Our Hard Rules are exactly the kind that get violated when the codebase grows fastest if enforcement is human-only. Critical-four-first respects R-08 (no overbuild before client #1). Seed import-linter contract + hooks installed at decision time. |
| D-30 | Backend construction patterns | BACKEND-PATTERNS.md adopted as a CORE BLUEPRINT doc (reading order #4, alongside TRD/DATA-MODEL — the construction manual for the whole backend build; this entry only records its adoption). Prescriptive for apps/api, voice-runtime and workers: module anatomy (routes/service/schemas, NO repository layer, one-test-file-per-behavior), locked bootstrap order with raw-body preservation on webhook paths, RFC-9457 ladder with kind/retryable/fields extensions, the reliability triad (idempotency table + transactional outbox + webhook inbox, all claimed via conditional-UPDATE CAS), tolerant worker boot with /healthz/ready as the go-live gate, degradation_mode enum, audit hash chain on audit_log, failure_stage alert taxonomy, named metric recorders, coverage ratchet scoped to high-risk modules. Two new M1 tables: outbox_messages, webhook_inbox_events (+ idempotency_records). | Patterns lifted from the raghava-organics production backend (proven under live traffic) and confirmed against 2026 industry practice (service layer + DI, async SQLAlchemy, outbox with SKIP LOCKED-style claiming, effectively-exactly-once = at-least-once + idempotent consumers). Skips recorded with reasons: no repo layer (swap seams belong at external boundaries), no success envelope (RFC-9457 already decided), Clerk owns sessions (their rotation design is the self-host reference), their ops-config overlay is the reference for per-tenant BYOK key storage. |

| D-31 | Primary voice engine | **Bolna replaces ThinnestAI** (supersedes D-02's rented-engine pick; the adapter architecture and phase-2/3 legs of D-02 stand). ThinnestAI retired before any adapter code existed. No fallback engine is designated: engine risk is carried by the VoiceEngine adapter contract (normalized schemas + conformance suite keep a second adapter cheap), and D-02's phase-2 self-orchestration trajectory stands unchanged on its own triggers. Adoption is gated on the Bolna pilot scorecard: webhook behavior, Telugu quality (STT + KB multilingual mode), real-PSTN latency, BYOK platform fee ≤ ~₹1.5/min in writing, agency/sub-account confirmation, INR/GST billing. Conformance discipline continues with the `bolna` + `fake` adapters until a second rented adapter exists. | Aug 2026 due diligence: ThinnestAI = domain registered Feb 2026, zero verifiable customers, no SLA/status page/agency clause, unresponsive (no-showed the scheduled meeting). Bolna = YC F25 + $6.3M General Catalyst seed (Jan 2026), named production customers (Varun Beverages, Spinny, Snabbit; ~200K calls/day per TechCrunch), verified Sarvam saaras v3 + Telugu te-IN + free BYOK + Exotel/Plivo/Vobiz/Twilio guides + published 140/160 DLT runbook + Enterprise sub-accounts and India data-residency. Consequence accepted and closed: **Bolna webhooks are unsigned and at-most-once** (docs + OSS delivery code verified: single POST, no retry/timeout/signing, errors swallowed; only control = static egress IP 13.203.39.153) — webhook doctrine amended in TRD §5, SEC-COMP §5, SURFACES §3.1 and FLOWS §3: authenticity = source-IP allowlist + execution-id dedupe, and the List-Executions reconciliation poller is promoted from safety net to guarantee of record. |

| D-32 | Engine-platform evaluation: what is a decision variable, and what is not (reaffirms D-31; does not change it) | **Vapi ELIMINATED permanently.** **LiveKit Cloud recorded as the best-evidenced phase-2 self-orchestration target** (does NOT displace D-02's Pipecat-on-CPU plan; both are phase-2 candidates, decided at the phase-2 gate on measured evidence). **Bolna reaffirmed as primary for M1.** Evaluation doctrine going forward: (a) inputs identical across platforms — Sarvam rates, Exotel rates, the Gemini-3.x migration, and BYOK model QUALITY (incl. Telugu STT/TTS accuracy) — are NOT decision variables; only platform-differentiated properties are; (b) vendor latency numbers are marketing until we measure them on real PSTN (Bolna publishes an undefined "<300ms"; a third-party comparison inflated this to "sub-600ms end-to-end" — both unusable); (c) at launch volume, MONTHLY FLOORS dominate per-minute rates, so a no-floor prepaid model beats a lower headline rate. | Verified Aug 2026 (adversarial multi-agent verification of first-party pages). **Vapi:** its $0.05/min "Vapi Hosting" fee is a separate line item that survives full BYOK = ₹4.40/min orchestration tax alone, exceeding our entire ₹3–3.6/min all-in target before any Sarvam/Exotel second; AND it has no India region (SIP+RTP terminate US/EU only, forcing an India→US→India hairpin of +230–260ms/turn). Either fact disqualifies; together it is not close. **LiveKit Cloud:** ap-south (Mumbai) region VERIFIED; first-party maintained Sarvam plugin (saaras:v3 / bulbul:v3 / sarvam-105b) with true BYOK and no resale markup; turn detection AND barge-in ship in the SDK by default. But its "$0.01/min" is two meters on one telephony minute (agent $0.01 + third-party SIP $0.004 ≈ $0.014 marginal), concurrency is hard-capped 5/20/600 — our 10–20 target sits exactly at the Ship ceiling with a 10× cliff to $500/mo beyond it — and the $50/mo Ship floor makes it ₹4.40/min at 1,000 min/month, i.e. identical to Vapi at OUR launch volume; the advantage appears only at ≥5,000 min/month. **Bolna wins the rows that bind now:** no monthly floor (prepaid credits, $5 free to start), 100 concurrent on Pilots with 250+ concurrent proven in production by two independent customers (GoKwik, Futwork), 8 named customers, campaigns/KB/post-call extraction/sub-accounts/DLT guidance included (4–8 engineering weeks we do not have pre-client-#1), and fastest time to revenue. Its known costs stay: unsigned webhooks, unpublished BYOK fee, US-region recordings pre-Enterprise, medium lock-in — all carried as pilot gates (OPERATIONS §2). **Telugu turn-detection finding (generalizes):** LiveKit's turn detector covers 14 languages incl. Hindi, EXCLUDING Telugu, and on an unsupported language it disables model-based end-of-utterance entirely rather than falling back (VAD-only silence timers). Endpointing is an ORCHESTRATION property that BYOK models do NOT fix — so Indic turn-taking is a build-and-verify line item on every DIY path, and a measured gate on every rented one (OPERATIONS §2 gate 5). |

| D-33 | Memory horizons + KB integration under BYOK (refines D-28; does not change it) | **Three memory horizons, named and separated** (TRD §6.1): **H1 in-call working memory** = the running dialogue, held by the ENGINE's orchestrator, discarded at hangup — not a component we build, buy or key, and not a BYOK slot; **H2 call-level durable memory** = transcript/summary/extraction/sentiment written by our existing post-call pipeline (TRD §8) into Postgres; **H3 caller/tenant long memory** = the D-28 managed service, injected PRE-call via the context webhook (~5s budget), never mid-call. The H1→H2 hand-off is the post-call pipeline; from H2 onward this is entirely mechanisms we already have. **KB under BYOK: the knowledge base is NOT a BYOK slot** — BYOK covers STT/TTS/LLM only, and no vector store of ours can be plugged into the engine's retrieval pipeline. Built-in KB (`rag_id`) requires no BYOK and carries no KB line on the pricing page → **inferred included in the platform fee (confirm in writing, gates 8 + 12)**. External KB is a different architecture (engine custom function → our endpoint → provider), not a different key. **v1 keeps in-call retrieval on the built-in KB** (reaffirms D-28's in-call gate, now with Bolna-specific numbers). Also: engine references inside D-28's text read as Bolna per D-31. | Founder question (Aug 2026), answered from first-party evidence. **Cost is not the deciding variable:** an external KB adds ≈₹0.02–0.05/min against a ₹1.75–2.30/min BYOK model stack — noise. **Latency is:** the external route adds two network hops (engine → our endpoint → provider and back) ≈ +150–400ms against a 100ms budget, and Bolna documents **no tool-call timeout at all**, so the ceiling is unmeasured. The built-in KB has zero network hops from the orchestrator. Its two known costs are carried as gates, not assumptions: Bolna's multilingual KB mode names Hindi and Tamil but **not Telugu**, and the mode is **immutable at KB creation** (gate 8) — if Telugu retrieval is poor, the custom-function route becomes the fallback and we accept the round trip, masked by the T3 filler utterance. Dual-push ingestion (engine KB + managed service) and the preview-and-approve gate stay ours regardless, so the choice is reversible. Corollary on H1 economics: the full conversation is resent to the LLM every turn, so in-call token cost grows through the call — the ₹0.15–0.20/min LLM figure is a blended average that long calls skew above; whether Bolna truncates/summarises history or enables provider context caching on BYOK keys is UNVERIFIED (gate 8). |

| D-39 | Build doctrine for the dual-motion era: **schema for scale, surface thin** — and **measure latency before optimising it** (founder delegated both calls) | **(a) SCOPE.** Resolve the "thin slice vs million-dollar SaaS" tension by splitting it: build the **data model and boundaries** for the end state in M1 — orgs/memberships/invites, credits + prepaid ledger, per-tenant metering, plan/tier column, engine adapter, RLS on every table — but ship the **narrow surface** first (admin-driven onboarding for client #1). Self-serve UI, campaigns and the credit top-up flow land M2. Rule of thumb: **anything that would require a migration or a re-meter later is built now; anything that is only a screen is built when a user needs it.** D-12 already establishes the precedent (metering is not retrofittable). **(b) LATENCY.** For M1, optimise *inside* Bolna and do not plan self-orchestration: we have **zero real-PSTN measurements**, so any optimisation now is guesswork. Sequence is measure → budget → optimise: pilot gate 4 produces p50/p95, then the cheap application-layer levers do most of the perceived-latency work — T0 compiled context (answers ~80% with no retrieval), filler-utterance masking on every tool call, a hard in-call tool budget, prompt-size discipline, and streaming the first sentence. Phase-2 self-orchestration stays gated on its existing numeric triggers (TRD §10). | **(a)** A 2-person team pre-client-#1 cannot afford either failure mode: shipping a thin slice that needs a rewrite to scale, or building a platform before anyone pays for it. Splitting on "migration cost" is the cheapest correct line. **(b)** Honest constraint recorded so it is not re-litigated: on a **rented** engine the audio path is largely Bolna's, so **we cannot beat a competitor on latency by platform choice** — if Outpero is also on Bolna (unresolved, teardown §10.3), our structural latency is identical to theirs. Our real levers are application-layer, and they are cheap; a genuine structural latency advantage requires phase-2 self-orchestration, which also removes the BYOK-keys-with-vendor exposure (TRD §5). Do not promise a latency edge in sales copy until gate 4 numbers exist. |

| D-37 | Auth: **keep Clerk — do NOT build our own** (reaffirms D-07/D-10 against a founder proposal to self-build, now that self-serve is in scope) | Two Clerk applications stay: **admin realm (invite-only, signup disabled)** and **client realm (self-serve signup: email/password + Google OAuth + admin invite links)**. Clerk **Organizations** back tenant membership and invitations; every user and org is **mirrored into our Postgres via Clerk webhooks**, so `organizations`/`users` remain OUR system of record and RLS keeps keying off our `tenant_id` — Clerk authenticates, it does not own our data model. Custom domain on `accounts.calevate.tech` so the flow is ours end to end. | **Verified pricing (clerk.com/pricing, 11 Aug 2026) removes the cost argument entirely:** free tier = **50,000 monthly retained users per app**, **unlimited applications**, **custom domains included**, and **100 monthly retained organizations** free (≤20 members each). Beyond 100 orgs, ~**$1/org/mo** (101–1,000 band); Pro is $25/mo. Concretely: **0–100 client businesses cost us ₹0**; 500 clients ≈ ₹35k/mo against ₹1,899–30,000/mo of revenue *per client*. Building our own would save ~₹0–2k/mo today and cost **3–6 engineering weeks pre-client-#1 plus permanent security ownership** of sessions, password hashing, MFA, recovery, OAuth, rotation, CSRF and rate-limiting. **The decisive argument is not cost, it is blast radius:** Hard Rule 1's RLS model trusts `tenant_id` from a verified session, so an auth defect is a cross-tenant data breach, not a login bug — that is the last surface a 2-person team should hand-roll before its first client. The founder's actual requirement ("directly integrated with Calevate") is satisfied without owning the crypto: custom domain, our branding, our DB as source of truth, our invite flow. Reversibility is already documented — D-30 records Clerk's session-rotation design as our self-host reference if we ever outgrow it. Revisit only if org count passes ~2,000 or an enterprise client demands SAML we cannot buy. |

| D-38 | Inbound receptionist is the **headline differentiator** | Ship the inbound 24/7 receptionist as a first-class channel and lead with it in positioning, rather than treating it as parity with outbound. It stays inside the same agent model (one agent = one channel, per D-21's config boundaries) and reuses the same script/extraction/compliance machinery. | Outpero **markets inbound but has not shipped it** — their own docs state inbound employees "can't be created yet" and an inbound-configured number "plays a short message rather than holding a full live conversation, even if your setup looks complete on screen" (teardown §9c). It is their only confirmed unbuilt flank, it is already in BRD §4.1 scope, and it is the highest-value use case for the SMBs we target (missed calls after hours = lost revenue they can feel). It also plays to our compliance edge: inbound is consent-clean by construction (the caller initiated), which sidesteps the DLT/DNC exposure that constrains outbound. |

| D-36 | **CANONICAL M1 STACK (locked)** — supersedes the model picks in D-04/D-20 on the LLM leg; D-31 (engine) and D-05 (telephony) unchanged | **Orchestration: Bolna (BYOK)** · **Telephony: Exotel** (140 via Vobiz / 160 via Plivo per their DLT runbook) · **STT: Sarvam Saaras (STT+Translate), ₹30/hr** · **LLM: Sarvam 105B — free per token (DEFAULT; replaces Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite)** · **TTS: Sarvam Bulbul v3 default, v2 as the value tier** (₹30 vs ₹15 per 10k chars). Gemini 2.5 Flash-Lite is **retained as a configurable fallback**, not the default — it stays the reference for post-call extraction quality until the Sarvam LLM is measured against the golden-transcript fixtures. Target all-in **≈₹3.1–3.6/min**; verified floor ₹2.9 on v2+Sarvam. | Three reasons, in priority order. **(1) Data residency, not cost** — SEC-COMP §4 already flags that Gemini sends transcript text to Google and that an "all-India mode" was a client option at a quality tradeoff; with Sarvam's LLM free *and* sovereign, that tradeoff mostly disappears and all-India becomes our DEFAULT posture. This is a live differentiator: Outpero's own privacy policy admits "some providers may process data on servers located outside India" (teardown §9b). **(2) Cost** — the LLM leg goes ₹0.15–0.20 → ₹0.00 and R-04's forced Gemini-3.x step (~₹0.55–0.65/min) is avoided entirely (D-35). **(3) Vendor simplicity** — one commercial relationship for STT+LLM+TTS, one rate card, one rotation. Accepted risks, each with a mitigation: Sarvam LLM quality on Telugu function-calling/extraction is UNMEASURED → keep Gemini configurable and run the golden-transcript fixtures before switching the extraction path; Sarvam rate limits (60/200/1,000 rpm by plan) are now a CONCURRENCY input, not a price input → size the plan at pilot gate 13; "free per token" may be promotional → confirm in writing, and the fallback is a ₹0.15–0.20/min step, not a redesign. Single-vendor concentration is the real cost of this choice — mitigated by keeping Cartesia as a second TTS candidate (D-35) and Gemini as a second LLM. |

| D-35 | Sarvam rate card verified live (corrects D-20 and R-04's premise) | Read from `sarvam.ai/api-pricing`, 11 Aug 2026. **(a) Sarvam 105B/30B chat LLM is FREE PER TOKEN** — the long-standing open question is closed, and R-04's "forced Gemini-3.x migration costs ~₹0.55–0.65/min" is now an *avoidable* step rather than an inevitable one; Gemini becomes a quality choice, not a cost necessity. **(b) Bulbul v2 is NOT discontinued** — it is live at **₹15/10,000 chars, half the v3 rate**; D-20 recorded it as "appears discontinued," which was wrong (that read came from ThinnestAI's docs listing, not from Sarvam). D-20's *choice* of V3 for quality still stands as the default, but v2 is now a deliberate cost lever. **(c)** STT ₹30/hr and Bulbul v3 ₹30/10k chars are confirmed unchanged. **(d)** No monthly minimum; ₹1,000 free credits; credits never expire and are universal across APIs; **the binding constraint is RATE LIMIT, not price** — 60 rpm Starter / 200 rpm Pro (₹10k) / 1,000 rpm Business (₹50k), which is a concurrency input for FLOWS §5 and pilot gate 13. | Consequence: the cheapest verified BYOK stack is **Bulbul v2 + Sarvam LLM ≈ ₹1.04–1.31/min**, ~45% below the v3+Gemini combination we had been planning against (₹1.73–2.32). This does not change the default model choice — Telugu quality decides that, by ear, at the pilot — but it gives us a real value/premium ladder built from published rates, mirroring how Outpero's ₹3/₹5/₹7 voice tiers work (TRD §10.3). Full recomputed model in TRD §10.1. |

| D-34 | Pricing collision with Outpero's self-serve product (OPEN — revisits D-11's evidence, does not yet change D-11) | An authenticated in-product teardown of Outpero (Aug 2026, `docs/evidence/outpero-teardown-aug2026.md`, BRD §5) found they now ship a **self-serve product at ₹0 setup + ₹1,899/employee/mo + ₹3–7/min** (voice-tier priced, 30-sec-block billing, volume-bonus credits, Enterprise waives the hire fee). D-11's stated rationale cites "Outpero's ₹60k+ setup pricing norms" as an anchor — that evidence is now **stale**: the ₹60k+ figure lives only on their high-touch agency packages, while the product an SMB actually shops costs ~₹2,241 first month vs our ₹55–105k. **RESOLVED 11 Aug 2026 (founder): option (b) — run BOTH motions.** (1) **Managed tier** keeps D-11 unchanged (setup ₹40–75k + retainer, we curate schemas, run QA, own compliance) — this is the client-#1 path and where the margin is. (2) **Self-serve tier** added beneath it: sign-up with **email/password + Google OAuth**, plus **admin-generated invite links** from the admin console; prepaid credits, per-minute talk-time, no setup fee. The two motions share one product and one tenancy model — a self-serve org is the same `organizations` row as a managed one, distinguished by a plan/tier column, so nothing forks. **Consequence for build order:** the *schema* for credits, metering, orgs, memberships and invites lands in M1 (D-12 already says metering is not retrofittable); the self-serve *UI and signup flow* land in M2. **New risk accepted — self-serve + Indian telecom compliance is the sharp edge** (anyone can sign up and dial): mitigations are non-negotiable and ship with the self-serve flow — platform-fixed calling hours, DNC scrub on every dispatch path (Hard Rule 5), mandatory non-null AI disclosure, a durable consent-attestation ledger, per-account concurrency and spend caps, and number provisioning gated behind KYC. Tracked as **R-11**. | The teardown also confirmed OpenDG (Outpero's parent) is a 2014 Hyderabad agency executing our exact service→product thesis, and that two July-2026 "gaps we exploit" (no QA process; not schema-driven) are now closed by them — so the competitive case rests on typed extraction, hard compliance/DPDP, engine isolation, closed-loop QA, and an inbound receptionist they have not shipped. Stack verified as React+Supabase+FastAPI-style REST on Sarvam+Cartesia (supersedes the OmniDimension-class inference). |

| D-40 | The webhook inbox's unit of work is the **transition**, not the execution | Key `webhook_inbox_events.event_key` on `{execution_id}:{raw_status}`, matching `job_id_for(INGEST_JOB, engine, execution_id, raw_status)`, which had assumed this since it was written. One durable inbox row per status change; a repeat delivery of the same transition is still one job. | Bolna fires one webhook per status change (queued → in-progress → completed, TRD §5) carrying the same execution id. Keying the inbox on the execution alone made the inbox and the job id disagree: the FIRST transition claimed the row and enqueued, and every later one — including `completed`, the only transition where cost, recording and transcript exist — came back `duplicate` and enqueued nothing. The post-call pipeline therefore never ran from a webhook at all: every call waited for the 10-minute reconciliation poller, which makes FLOWS §3.6's "lead + summary visible < 2 min after hangup" unmeetable and turns D-31's "poller = guarantee of record, webhook = low-latency hint" into "the poller does everything". `pipeline.py` returning `awaiting_completion:{raw_status}` was the other half of the evidence. No migration: `event_key` is Text under a `(provider, event_key)` unique constraint. |

| D-41 | The KB **detach contract**: a published knowledge version must be withdrawable, so `attach_kb` returns the engine's handle and the protocol gains `detach_kb` + `list_kb` | `VoiceEngine.attach_kb(ref, source)` now returns `EngineKBRef` — the vendor's own id for its copy (Bolna: `rag_id`), opaque above the adapter exactly like `EngineAgentRef`. Two methods join the Protocol: `detach_kb(ref, kb)` removes ONE attached source, `list_kb(ref)` reads back what the agent actually holds. The handle is recorded in `kb_documents.meta.engine_kb_ref` on the source's first chunk — the home the KB migration already designated for provider-side ids — so this costs no column and no migration. Publish order in `apps/api/kb/service.py::publish_source` is **detach-then-attach**, and **a failed detach ABORTS the publish, leaving the previously approved version live**. | Our `kb_sources.id` addresses nothing on the engine's side: the engine names its own copy, so `DELETE /knowledgebase/<our uuid>` would 404 forever while looking like a working detach. Without a returned handle a published document can never be retracted — which is exactly what went wrong: "supersede" only ever happened in OUR tables, so a superseded knowledge base stayed live on the agent, which kept answering from v1 while every screen reported v2. That divergence between the published KB and the approved one is the single thing the FLOWS §7 approval gate exists to prevent, and after a rollback it was worse — every version attached at once. **Failure policy, with both alternatives rejected on the record.** Publishing over a version we could not withdraw is the original defect (two versions live, the agent free to quote the old prices, our tables reporting success). Dropping the old and publishing nothing is an outage we caused to avoid an inconsistency. Refusing keeps the client whole: their agent still answers, from text a human approved; what they lose is the UPDATE, and the retry costs nothing because nothing has been attached yet. A live version we hold no handle for is the same refusal for the same reason (`kb_engine_ref_unknown`) — we must not publish over what we cannot address; the remediation is to withdraw the stale copy vendor-side once, not to weaken the rule. The detach must be REAL: an adapter that accepts the call and does nothing turns publish into a silent lie nothing downstream can detect, so the conformance suite observes removal through `list_kb` rather than trusting the call, and detaching a handle the engine never issued must RAISE. Accepted cost of the ordering: between detach and attach the agent holds no copy of that source and answers T4 "I don't know" — one request of silence is cheaper than a stale price the client is then held to. Two vendor facts stay UNVERIFIED and are carried as pilot gate 8 questions (OPERATIONS §2), not assumptions: whether the KB list response carries the agent linkage `list_kb` filters on, and whether deleting a KB also clears the agent's reference to it. **Both now have an executable probe** (`scripts/pilot/knowledge.py`, tested in `tests/pilot_knowledge_test.py`), and what each will answer is fixed in advance so the result cannot be argued about afterwards. (a) LINKAGE, answered behaviourally — attach to two agents, list both, and if both lists come back empty, DELETE the handle `attach_kb` returned: a delete the engine ACCEPTS proves the knowledge base existed and the list simply did not attribute it, so the answer is "linkage absent or renamed" and the consequence is that `list_kb` today reports EVERY agent as holding no knowledge base — a cross-tenant wrong answer that looks like a working feature, and D-41's detach read-back proves nothing until the adapter filters on the real field. A delete the engine REFUSES leaves the question INCONCLUSIVE (a broken attach cannot testify about a list), and one agent's handle appearing in the other's list is the worst answer of the four: the list is not agent-scoped at all. Reading the row's fields directly is NOT available to the probe — that is a vendor shape, so it stays inside `apps/api/engine/` (hard rule 2) and the raw capture belongs to the fixture-recording slice. (b) DANGLING REF: if the agent still references the deleted `rag_id`, `detach_kb` grows a SECOND call (an agent update) and stays mandatory — the detach-then-attach ordering above is unchanged, its first step becomes two operations. Our Protocol has no agent read-back (`create_agent`/`update_agent` only), so the probe answers this only when the runner supplies an agent read-back or a live call, and otherwise reports INCONCLUSIVE with that gap named rather than guessing. |

| D-42 | Outbox claim exclusivity is a **lease on the row** (`locked_until`), not a fourth `status` value | `outbox_messages` gains a nullable `locked_until timestamptz` (migration `7c04ab5f9e26`; no default, no backfill, catalog-only DDL). `claim_outbox_batch` commits the `attempt_count` bump and the lease in one statement on its own connection; the claim predicate becomes `status = 'pending' AND (locked_until IS NULL OR locked_until <= now()) ORDER BY created_at, id`. `status` keeps its three values, and `OUTBOX_CLAIM_LEASE` is 2 minutes — its own constant, not the 10-minute `CLAIM_LEASE`, because a claimed outbox row is held for milliseconds. No new index. | The bug it fixes: the attempt bump lived inside the dispatcher's transaction, which does not commit until after the publish, so a SIGKILL (OOM kill, container eviction, a deploy that does not wait) rolled it back and the row returned to `pending` with `attempt_count = 0`. A message whose payload is what kills the worker therefore looped forever, on a retry budget that reset every pass, toward a DLQ it could never reach — and silently, because the evidence died with the process. There is no fix without a COMMIT, and committing the bump alone is a different bug: the commit drops the `FOR UPDATE` locks that were the only thing making the claim exclusive. Exclusivity has to survive the commit, which means moving it out of the lock and onto a durable fact. Lease over status on four counts. (1) **It adds no state anyone has to learn** — the `AND status = 'pending'` CAS guards in `mark_outbox_published`/`mark_outbox_failed`, the outbox-lag metric, the DLQ-depth query and `replay_dead_letters` all stay correct untouched; a `claimed` status changes the CHECK constraint and puts every one of them in this migration's blast radius. (2) **Recovery needs no new machinery** — a lapsed lease is picked up by the claim query the dispatchers already run each tick, with `attempt_count` where the dead worker left it, so the recovery path IS the normal path; an abandoned `claimed` row needs a reaper with its own schedule, its own failure mode and its own argument about how long is too long. (3) **It is the lease this module already runs on** (`CLAIM_LEASE`, idempotency and inbox claims), with the deadline STATED rather than inferred from `updated_at`, which every status transition also bumps and which therefore answers the wrong question. (4) **The deciding one: a mixed-version deploy degrades to the old behaviour instead of to a stall.** An old dispatcher ignoring the lease can double-publish — which `job_id_for(job, message.id)` already dedupes at the queue, and which is exactly what the system did before this migration; the same overlap under a `claimed` status strands rows in a state old code never selects and silently no-ops against. Choosing a failure mode we already survive is the only honest thing to do to a queue carrying a client's CRM notifications. What the status value is genuinely better at is legibility, and that is bought back for one predicate on one screen (`locked_until > now()`). No index, deliberately: `ix_outbox_pending (status, created_at)` already walks pending rows in order and stops when the batch is full, while an index leading `(status, locked_until, …)` cannot deliver that ordering for an OR predicate and would force a sort of the whole pending set to answer a LIMIT. |

| D-43 | **Calevate's own telemarketer registration is ONE row on `platform_state`**, not a column on every tenant | Four columns on the single global row (migration `d7f2a3c9b410`): `tm_registration_status` NOT NULL DEFAULT `not_registered` under a five-value CHECK (`not_registered`, `submitted`, `active`, `suspended`, `revoked`), `tm_id`, `tm_registered_at`, `tm_verified_at`, plus a CHECK that `active` requires both a `tm_id` and a `tm_registered_at`. Read by `campaigns.service.launch_blockers` as **`tm_registration_missing`**, written only through `POST /v1/ops/platform/tm-registration` (`ops:manage`, step-up confirmed in both directions, audited in the same transaction). `platform_state` carries no `tenant_id`, so hard rule 1 does not reach it and no RLS exemption entry is needed. | It is ONE fact about ONE entity: **us**. Copied per tenant it becomes N facts that drift — a client onboarded in March saying we are registered and one onboarded in July saying we are not — and the launch gate's answer would then depend on which copy it happened to read. So it sits beside the load-shed mode and the big red switch, which answer the same question ("is this platform allowed to place calls right now"), and every tenant's launch preview reads the same value. **The read deliberately bypasses the load-shed cache.** `core.loadshed` reads `platform_state` through a three-layer cache with a 15-second window, which is right for a mode consulted on every request and wrong here: a compliance fact checked once per launch must not be 15 seconds stale, because a registration revoked ten seconds ago must block the campaign being launched right now. `read_tm_registration` therefore runs on the caller's own session — one query on a connection that is already open, and never stale. **And it fails CLOSED, the opposite of that cache's deliberate fail-open.** A missing row makes `loadshed` answer `normal`, because a fresh database is not an emergency and refusing every request over an absent seed row would be a self-inflicted outage; an unreadable registration, by contrast, is not evidence of a registration, and the cost of guessing wrong is dialling India's network as an unregistered telemarketer. No row, no registration. **Seeded `not_registered`, deliberately**: R-01 is precisely that our TM registration requires an entity we are still standing up, and seeding `active` would mean inventing a registration number — which the second CHECK makes impossible to do by accident. The consequence is intended: on a fresh database no campaign launches until ops records the registration. That is not a regression, it is the gate finally being able to ask. |

| D-44 | **The erasure record stops being the last copy of the number**: `deletion_requests.phone_e164` is cleared on completion and `subject_ref` takes over | Migration `f4a8e1c07b62`. `subject_ref TEXT NOT NULL` (sha256 of the number, hex, first 32 chars) is backfilled, then `phone_e164` drops NOT NULL and every completed row is cleared. Two CHECKs — an OPEN request must still name its subject (`completed_at IS NOT NULL OR phone_e164 IS NOT NULL`) and `subject_ref IS NOT NULL` — plus `INDEX (tenant_id, subject_ref)` and a BEFORE INSERT trigger that fills the reference when a writer did not, and never overwrites. `retention.execute_deletion_request` nulls the number in the SAME UPDATE that stamps `completed_at` and `proof`; `compliance/deletion.py` writes `subject_ref` on insert and reads it back rather than re-deriving it. `phone_e164` is NOT dropped (hard rule 8). | After a successful DPDP erasure the last place the person's number survived was the record proving we removed it — and no retention policy sweeps `deletion_requests` at all, so that copy was not merely surviving, it was permanent. **Clearing it in the same UPDATE as the proof is the whole design**: there is then no window in which a request is complete and still holds its subject. It cannot be cleared any earlier, because the worker's only handle on the subject IS that row — it resolves the number from it and the outbox payload deliberately carries ids only, so a queue dump is not a list of people who asked to be forgotten (hard rule 6). What clearing costs is the question support actually gets asked — "have we already erased this person?" — and `subject_ref` buys it back: it is the identical construction to the erasure proof's `subject_hash` and the subject-access export's `subject_ref`, which is why all three still line up, so an access request and an erasure for the same person stay correlatable without any record carrying the number. The hash is not reversible, and that asymmetry is the point: it answers questions ABOUT a number you already have, and nothing at all to a reader holding the table and not the number. The OPEN-request dedupe keeps using `phone_e164` (`uq_deletion_requests_open_subject`, migration `e2c47b90d5a1`) because an open row always carries it — now a CHECK rather than an assumption — and NULLs never conflict in a unique index, so making the column nullable WITHOUT that CHECK would have let a cleared-too-early row silently opt out of the guarantee the previous migration had just established. **The DOWNGRADE deletes completed rows**, stated here rather than discovered at 3am: restoring NOT NULL requires a number, a cleared number cannot be reconstituted from a hash, and a rollback that reconstituted it would be a fresh breach. The rows that survive a revert are the open ones, which are the only ones the pre-migration code needs. |

| D-45 | **An ops step-up confirmation names the TRANSITION, not the endpoint** | `X-Confirm-Action` on `POST /v1/ops/platform` must echo the exact move being made: `halt_outbound`, `release_outbound`, `set_load_shed:<mode>`, or — for a request that does both — the two joined in a fixed order (`halt_outbound+set_load_shed:reduced`). The string is built in ONE place, `ops.routes.platform_confirmation`, which the runbooks quote and `tests/platform_halt_test.py` pins literal by literal. The single generic string `set_platform_state` now authorises **nothing**, in either direction. A body that changes neither field is refused `platform_state_no_change` rather than reaching the writer, changing nothing and still writing an audit row for a platform change nobody made. | One string across three moves meant a header captured for the smallest authorised the largest: a confirmation an operator sent for a routine Tuesday load-shed tweak also satisfied **lifting the global outbound halt**, which is the single action that puts every tenant's dialling back on the network. BACKEND-PATTERNS §7 asks for the confirmation to be bound to the specific action, and the spend-cap recompute had always done it properly — its string carries the tenant id, so a header sent for one client cannot be replayed against another. The load-shed string carries its TARGET MODE for exactly that reason: `reduced` is a routine change and `maintenance` sheds reads, and consent to one is not consent to the other. A combined request is strictly more dangerous than either half, so both halves must be confirmed rather than either alone. **Breaking on purpose, and deliberately not grandfathered**: the old string's whole defect was that it authorised more than the operator meant, so accepting it "for compatibility" would keep the hole open under a different name. The cost is real and is paid down rather than denied — an operator's muscle-memory curl now 403s, so the refusal names the exact header to repeat with in its `remediation`, and both callers moved in the same change (the console's `useSetPlatformState`, and `runbooks/campaign-stall.md` §1). What this is NOT: a second factor. A header the caller supplies is not authentication and is not pretending to be — it stops the accidental and the drive-by until the admin realm's Clerk re-auth binding replaces it (TRD §2), and it is here now because adding it later would mean changing the callers, which is the cost this entry just finished paying. |

| D-46 | **A tenant's plan is the row whose EFFECTIVE WINDOW contains the instant** — not the newest row | `plans.effective_from` / `effective_to` become the row's valid time, resolved through one helper (`billing/plans.plan_in_effect_sql`) that every money reader now calls: the derived invoice, the usage panel, both cap reads, the worst-case call-cost quote and the dispatch concurrency ceiling. The period is **half-open `[from, to)`** per SQL:2011 application-time period tables, and the instant is PASSED IN rather than assumed to be now — a closed month prices at its last instant, the current month at now, a future month at its first instant (`month_pricing_instant`). One plan per month; **no proration**. The SQL:2011 non-overlap constraint (`PERIOD … WITHOUT OVERLAPS`; on pg16, `EXCLUDE USING gist` + `btree_gist`) is **DECLINED**: instead, resolution is a **total order** — latest `effective_from` wins (NULL sorting as `-infinity`), then `created_at DESC, id DESC`. A tenant with plan rows and none in effect is **UNPRICED** (no fee, no included minutes, no rate, no ceiling, default concurrency) and logs `plan_window_leaves_tenant_unpriced`. | The two columns were declared, migrated and read by nothing while every reader took `ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1`. Those are not the same rule, and the gap was a money bug in both directions: a price change staged for next month re-priced TODAY's bill, panel, quote and ceiling the moment the row was inserted; and because an invoice here is a DERIVED statement rather than a stored row, re-rendering July after any plan change re-priced July — a statement that changes when you look at it twice is not a statement. **Half-open is adopted verbatim because closed-closed makes the changeover instant belong to both rows**, so the day a plan is superseded is priced by a coin flip; with `[from, to)` the obvious operator gesture (`old.effective_to = new.effective_from`) is exactly right, with no gap and no double-priced instant. **The exclusion constraint was refused on evidence, not taste**: every `plans` row that exists today is NULL/NULL — an unbounded window — so any two of them overlap and the constraint would refuse the table's own contents; `billing/caps.py::apply_client_caps` MINTS a windowless row for a tenant with no plan so the client's own cap has somewhere to live, and that must keep working; and NULL bounds are not a defect to be migrated away — `effective_from IS NULL` means "since forever" and `effective_to IS NULL` means "until further notice", which is what an open-ended retainer actually is. So overlap is resolved deterministically rather than forbidden, and the order has two properties worth stating: a dated row beats an undated one once it comes into effect (so the ordinary gesture — leave the old windowless row alone, INSERT the new terms with a start date — is correct without a second statement), and **with every window NULL the order collapses to `created_at DESC, id DESC`, the newest-row rule this repo already had — so this re-prices nobody on the day it lands.** The `id DESC` tail is the same load-bearing tail as `ix_credit_ledger_tenant_recent`: "newest" has to be a TOTAL order or two readers disagree about which row it is, and two rows inserted in one transaction share a `created_at`. **The consequence recorded rather than discovered:** an operator who sets `effective_to` on a live plan WITHOUT inserting a successor drops that tenant's ceiling and overage rate at that instant, silently. That is deliberate — falling back to the expired row would charge a client at terms whose end date we were explicitly told, which is the one thing an end date must prevent — so instead both money readers call `warn_no_plan_in_effect` and the misconfiguration gets a log line an operator can act on rather than a quietly free month. Corollary: `?month=` is now strict (`invalid_billing_month`, 422). A month string selects WHICH PLAN prices the answer, so a month we cannot parse is one we cannot honestly price, and a 422 beats a ₹0.00 invoice for `?month=july`. |

| D-47 | **Subscriber KYC is two gates with two different scopes** — and no identity document is stored anywhere | `kyc_records` (migration `a3f6b1e02d95`; one mutable row per tenant, standard §1 RLS, FORCEd) records that a person at Calevate verified a business's identity against a named **public business-registry** document, on a date, with the evidence pack filed under a reference. **Dialling** is gated for `self_serve` / `trial` only — `compliance.service.kyc_blocker` returns `kyc_missing` / `kyc_not_verified`, asked identically by `check_dispatch` (per dial) and `campaigns.service.launch_blockers` (launch preview). **Buying a number** — `POST /v1/numbers/purchase` — is gated for **every** tier, with no `plan_tier` test at all. Ops records the verification at `POST /v1/admin/tenants/{tenant_id}/kyc` (`admin:tenants`, audited); the client reads their own at `GET /v1/compliance/kyc` and there is deliberately **no client-realm write**. Inbound is untouched (D-38).
