shares one bucket — so it fires the same `alert()` path the receiver uses for the identical condition. **Two of my own tests passed for the wrong reason and were fixed rather than reported**: the limiter suites drew bucket subjects from RFC 5737's 254-address /24 against hour-long windows, so a later run inherits an earlier run's count, and a production 60s test window can roll mid-flood and silently reset the counter — replaced by call-unique RFC 3849 IPv6 subjects, a unique trusted peer per call and an hour-long test window, verified stable over eight consecutive runs in one minute. Fourteen sabotages, each with its own signature; the one worth naming is that removing the per-tenant limit from the PRODUCTION profile fails only the census test, never the behaviour tests — which patch the profile — so configuration and mechanism are guarded by different files on purpose. |
| D-132 | **§52 made decidable for the shapes that were hiding in it: the guard grows a failure-state gate, and the nine sites it found are closed — one of them a tenant erasure offered over a read that never landed** | `tests/surfaceStatesGuard.test.ts` gains three rules on top of its original two, all off the same `X.data`-still-includes-`undefined` mechanism: `Boolean(q.data?.flag)` treated as `?? false`; a manufactured literal ANYWHERE (not only in a JSX child); and a POSITIVE truthiness test that decides whether a CONTROL is rendered, following one hop through a `const` that still carries the `undefined` and treating an early-returning `if`'s fall-through as its else. The last two are gated on "nothing in the declaring function ever reads this query's `isError`/`error`/`status`", mutation envelopes are excluded, and the fixture grows five banned and six safe shapes. `EXEMPT` is now empty. Fixed: the admin lifecycle erasure panel (skeleton + `ProblemNotice` above the `filed.data?.[0]` branch); the lead-sources agent picker (refusal in place of the `<select>`, and the save blocked); the leads table (`readOnly` and `canCall` moved onto `useWriteAccess`, `unavailable` names which read did not answer); the call-detail follow-up card (loading and failed branches beside the eligible one); the dashboard's "Spend this month" tile (its own three-state ladder); the integrations payload offer (`useWriteAccess`, plus a new `WriteAccess.unknown` so "we could not check" can be said without printing a refusal at every staff reader); and both shell counters, whose `?? 0` made a dead API look like an all-clear. `BLOCKER_COPY` keeps precedence over the server's `reason` and gains `tests/campaignBlockerCopy.test.ts`, which reads the compliance gate's rule strings and fails naming any key that no longer matches one. | **The guard's own "not checked" list was an exact map of where the defects were**, and the narrowing that produced it was correct when written and wrong now: flagging every literal fallback measured 22 hits/~20 correct, the failure-state gate got it to 12/2, and it was rejected because the slice that wrote it owned none of those screens — an EXEMPT list of ten is a treadmill. **The treadmill was a property of the backlog, not of the rule**; this slice owns the screens, so the gate goes in and the list comes out empty. Re-measured with the gate plus two narrowings §52 itself supplies — positive polarity only (`if (!q.data) return <refusal/>` is the CORRECT spelling, and four screens fail closed on `q.data !== undefined`), and a branch that withholds a CONTROL rather than a sentence — it lands at **9 hits, 9 real**. The worst was `admin/.../lifecycle`: `useTenantErasures`'s `isLoading` and `error` were read nowhere, so during the read AND after it 503s the screen stated that no erasure had been filed and offered an irreversible tenant-wide DPDP erasure that may already have been running. **On `BLOCKER_COPY` the two offered fixes are not equivalent**: inverting precedence trades a hypothetical drift for a certain regression, because `launch_blockers` writes for an operator ("Campaign is running, not draft.", and `PURCHASED_LIST_REASON` cites "policy, SEC-COMP §3" at a client) and the three DLT-entity entries exist precisely to say whose desk it is on — the table is a translation keyed by the server's identifier, and only the KEY can go stale silently. The end state is neither fix and needs no test: type `BlockerOut.rule` as a `Literal[...]` union so `openapi.json` carries the enum and `tsc` checks it, exactly as `LIST_PROVENANCE_COPY` already is — a BACKEND change, reported not made. **Two of this slice's own tests proved nothing and were fixed**: the mutation-vs-query exclusion stayed green when neutered, because the fixture's mutation branch held a `<p>` rather than a control, and the guard's whole-tree scan was quadratic per component (13s, timing out under the parallel suite and starving two unrelated files into failure) until the refusal answer was memoised per declaration. Eighteen sabotages, each with its own signature; the one worth naming is that reverting a refusal a screen still mentions elsewhere is invisible to the guard and caught only by the screen's own test — the gate is per-query, not per-branch, and that is the price of not computing branch dominance. |
| D-133 | **Twenty-one routes had no HTTP-layer test, and driving them found three route wrappers where the database was the only refusal** | Five suites — `kb_review_routes_test`, `dlt_admin_routes_test`, `agent_publishing_route_wrappers_test`, `campaign_route_wrappers_test`, `admin_read_route_wrappers_test` — drive all 21 at the wire: the realm or the under-privileged role, the happy path with the RESPONSE MODEL'S FIELDS read rather than its status code, the `audit_log` row with its redacted summary, and the cross-tenant id. Four defects fixed. `campaigns.service.add_contacts` answered 422 `campaign_not_draft` — "Contacts can only be added before a campaign is launched" — to an id no campaign of the caller's holds; it now answers 404, the shape its three sibling readers of that column already had. `POST …/dlt-templates` and `POST …/kyc` answered **500 `internal_error`** to a mistyped tenant uuid, and `POST …/numbers` answered **409 `number_taken`** to one; all three now ask `service.tenant_exists`, the predicate whose own docstring exists for this. | **The census was re-derived rather than taken from the audit, and the audit's 27 is now 21**: three of its entries had gained HTTP tests in this wave (`endpoints/sheets`, both `leads/views` mutations), and my own first pass produced three false positives because its regex could not match a path built as `f"…/{source['id']}/rotate-secret"` — a quote inside an f-string hole. A route census by grep is a census with a spelling bug in it; the corrected script is the number reported. **The three tenant-uuid defects are one shape, not three bugs**: every one of these routes names its tenant in a PATH an operator copies off a console URL, and nothing stood between that string and an INSERT carrying it as a foreign key. The `number_taken` one is the worst of the three despite being the only non-500: `provision_number` maps EVERY `IntegrityError` to that code, which is correct for the UNIQUE index it was written for, so a typo told an operator to go looking for the other account holding a number nobody holds. **Requirement "403 under impersonation" was deliberately NOT written 18 times**: `realm_boundary_test::test_no_route_declaring_a_mutating_permission_is_reachable_while_impersonating` already DRIVES every mutating-permission route under a real minted grant, so per-route copies would be a second way to answer one question — each new file names that sweep instead, so the next reader does not add them. Five sabotages, five distinct signatures (`422 != 404`; `404 != 422` plus a pre-existing suite going red; two different `ForeignKeyViolation`s; `409 number_taken != 404`), each restored under `sha256sum -c` with a green baseline either side. |
| D-134 | **The Vertex client, one availability policy, and twenty-four doc claims bound to a constant instead of to prose** | `apps/workers/extraction.py`'s `GEMINI_CHAT_URL` pointed at `generativelanguage.googleapis.com` and authenticated with `params={'key': ...}`; both are gone. `VertexGeminiExtractor` posts to `vertex_generate_url()` — an f-string that fills the host prefix AND the `locations/` segment from ONE frozen constant, `calevate_shared.engine.VERTEX_LOCATION`, beside `SARVAM_DEFAULT_LLM` where this repo keeps model identity — with an OAuth2 service-account bearer, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (`GEMINI_DEFAULT_LLM`, GA 7 May 2026 and Google's named replacement for the 2.5 family that retires 16 Oct 2026, BRD R-04), and a Vertex `responseSchema` built from the agent's own `ExtractionSchemaSpec` so valid JSON is a MODEL-side guarantee rather than `_first_json_object`'s fence-stripping — which stays for Sarvam, whose API publishes `json_object` but no schema. `check_model_residency`'s dated allowance for `extraction.py` is DELETED, which its own `stale_allowances()` required. **G-6 is one function**: `assist_capability()` answers with a DISCLOSED Sarvam fallback (`fallback_reason` + the sentence the response and the screen must carry) or a refusal with a remediation, on authored reasons `no_credential` / `quota_exhausted` / `provider_unavailable` / `ai_studio_key_disqualified`, in `PaymentCapability`'s shape. `run_assist()` re-runs `redact()` on its input and REFUSES text that still matches, so G-2 is structural. New `Settings`: `gcp_project_id` (plain config, GCP's own 6-30 pattern) and `gcp_service_account_json` (secret-shaped, so `platform_secrets` seals it) — and NO region field, which `check_model_residency` check 3 enforces by name. `GEMINI_EXTRACTION_DEFAULT is False` is minted as the greppable form of G-7 and now binds eleven prose sites across CLAUDE.md, AGENTS, README, TRD (×3), BRD R-04, BUILD-LOG, OPERATIONS, settings.py and the evidence set through `check_docs_drift` §5. | **The branch that had to go was not the URL, it was the selector.** `get_extractor()` returned a Gemini client whenever a Gemini key was configured and a Sarvam key was not — and its caller is `workers/pipeline.py`, which hands over the RAW transcript (`turn.text`, one line after `redacted.text`, deliberately, because a CRM callback-number field needs the digits). So ONE absent environment variable sent raw caller PII to a second processor, which is G-2 inverted by configuration rather than by code. Fixing the host and leaving that ladder would have shipped a correctly-regioned endpoint receiving data it may never see. Gemini is now unreachable from that function at all; a Google-only deployment extracts with `OfflineExtractor`, which is the intended direction and is a state no environment is in (`SARVAM_API_KEY` has been a `/healthz/ready` requirement since before D-127). **`google-auth` was resolved, its lockfile diff read (hard rule 9), and declined** — `google-auth 2.56.3` pulls `cryptography` (already here) plus `pyasn1-modules`/`pyasn1`, two pure-Python packages with no install hooks, so the supply-chain diff was honest and not the objection. So the flow was EXTRACTED to `apps/workers/google_oauth.py` and `google_sheets.py` migrated onto it in the same change — one implementation, keyed now on `(client_email, scope)` because one account serving two scopes was the one behaviour the move had to ADD (a token minted for `.../auth/spreadsheets` handed to Vertex is a 403 that names neither the scope nor the cache). `apps/workers` also gained the `pyjwt[crypto]` declaration it had been importing on `apps/api`'s: two lines in the lockfile, zero new packages. ⚠ **Evidence standing on the model: REPORTED, NOT READ.** `docs.cloud.google.com` is refused by this environment's egress proxy, so `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`'s GA date, its price and its retirement lineage rest on independent search summaries agreeing, and **that `asia-south1` SERVES it is NOT confirmed** — an OPERATIONS §2 gate on the day the GCP project exists, not an engineering unknown, because the failure is a 404 from a host that unambiguously serves our project and `assist_capability()` answers `provider_unavailable` with a disclosed Sarvam fallback meanwhile. **Not done, with the reason**: `llm_tok_in` — a `usage_events` CHECK-constraint value with no writer anywhere — is still there. Removing it means rewriting `ck_usage_events_unit_type_enum`, which migration `e1a7c93d5b02` (D-127 G-3's dashboard-AI unit types) is rewriting in this same wave and whose comment already names Part 18 as its owner; a second migration on the same constraint would collide in the alembic DAG and in the constraint body. Hard rule 8's step one (stop writing it) is trivially satisfied — nothing ever did — and step two belongs to the next migration that touches that constraint. |
| D-135 | **The adversarial pass: RLS is the whole of tenant isolation and every route proved it, but three limits the platform declares were not limits — a bucket the caller could rename, a body cap one header walked past, and a blocking socket read on the event loop that any stranger could aim** | `core/context.bearer_token` becomes the ONE reading of `Authorization` — leaf module, beside the header names, for the reason stated there — and `RateLimitMiddleware._caller` keys on it instead of on `fingerprint(<raw header>)`. `BodyLimitMiddleware` now COUNTS the streamed body as well as reading `Content-Length`, refusing mid-request by handing the app `http.disconnect` and dropping anything it sends afterwards, so the 413 is the caller's whole response. `verify_token`'s JWKS lookup moves to `asyncio.to_thread` and `PyJWKClient` gets `timeout=JWKS_FETCH_TIMEOUT_S` (5s) instead of PyJWT's 30. `tests/adversarial_pass_test.py` pins all three plus the IDOR sweep: 34 `{id}` routes in the client path space, driven with a NEIGHBOUR's real row ids and a valid session, required to answer 404 `not_found` and never 403, 422 or 2xx. | **What held is the headline and it was verified by taking it away**: with `ALTER ROLE calevate_app BYPASSRLS` the same sweep leaks 29 routes at once — reads another tenant's leads, calls, transcripts and campaigns, and DELETEs their lead source, their webhook endpoint and their members — so tenant isolation is the database's and nothing else's, which is the property that survives a refactor and the answer PART 6 asked for empirically. Also held under attack, with reproductions: `alg:none`, RS256→HS256 with the public key as the HMAC secret, an expired RS256 token and a wrong-realm token are all one indistinguishable 401; admin `fva[1]==-1` and an absent `fva` are 403s with different codes; CSV formula injection is disarmed in every cell including the phone column; deeply nested JSON is a 400 on the API path as well as on voice-runtime; the signup quota keys on the verified Clerk id and the resolved address, so header rotation buys nothing; no `text()` interpolation anywhere reaches user input; and email header injection is refused twice over (`EmailStr` at the boundary, `EmailMessage` raising on a linefeed). **The three that were real share one shape** — a control that names a number the caller can change. The limiter's bucket was a hash of the raw header, so `Bearer x`, `bearer x` and `Bearer x ` were three budgets and padding made it unbounded: one authenticated client could spend the whole `bulk_read` allowance as many times as it liked, which is the profile whose comment says it exists because "this is the shape data exfiltration takes". The body cap read `Content-Length`, and `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` declares none — the argument that nginx backstops it was checked and is false, `client_max_body_size` on the api vhost is **25m**, twelve times the app's 2 MiB. The JWKS fetch is `urllib.request.urlopen` (READ AT SOURCE, PyJWT 2.13.0) called straight from an `async def`, and `get_signing_key` refetches whenever the `kid` is unknown while never memoising the failure — so varying one field of an unsigned JWT buys one blocking fetch per request, and `/healthz*` has no limit profile at all because a probe must answer during an incident. Measured: 1s of JWKS latency was 1s in which the process served nobody. **The fix nearly broke hard rule 3 and the guardrail caught it**: `bearer_token` was written in `core/auth`, which `core/middleware` would then have imported — and `core/middleware` is on voice-runtime's pinned import surface while `apps.api.compliance`, which `core/auth` pulls, is FORBIDDEN on it. It lives in `core/context` for that reason, which is also where it belongs. **Handed back rather than fixed, both documented as deliberate**: `kb/service.preview` and `agents/prompts.list_prompt_versions` answer 200 `[]` for an id this tenant cannot see, where `kb.service.approve_source`'s own docstring says the honest answer is 404 — no data leaks (RLS empties both), but a client cannot tell "this source has no chunks" from "that is not your source", and `tests/kb_isolation_test.py:122` pins the weaker contract on purpose. |
| D-136 | **Six half-wired compliance routes get the affordances they were designed for, and the eval harness gets a provider dimension that refuses rather than scores zero** | **PART 9.** New ops screen `/admin/ops/dnc` (nav entry, a11y-swept) drives all three global-DNC routes: paste-and-suppress with the `suppress_number_platform_wide` header, the masked list, and a PER-ROW release confirmation that names the number and takes RELEASE typed against that row. `MAX_NUMBERS_PER_ADD`/`DNC_LIST_LIMIT`/`parsePastedNumbers` are IMPORTED from the client-realm `dnc.ts` rather than restated, and `WriteFailure` (the step-up-skew panel) moved out of `ops/page.tsx` into `admin/ops/writeFailure.tsx` with its old caller migrated in the same change. New client screen `/c/[slug]/settings/alerts` and a new operator panel on `/admin/tenants/[tenantId]` drive the three WhatsApp alert routes; the panel needed a read that did not exist, so `whatsapp_optin_routes` gains `GET /v1/admin/tenants/{tenant_id}/whatsapp-alerts` (`admin:tenants`, the owner's state, no number). `runbooks/dnc-complaint.md` §6 now names the screen and keeps the curl as the console-down fallback, and gains §8 — the full NCPR scrub procedure, which is the ONE route of the six that stays curl-only. **PART 16.** `scripts/eval.py` grows `--provider` (repeatable) over a `PROVIDERS` registry built on `vertex_extractor()`/`SarvamExtractor`/`OfflineExtractor`, a per-FIELD scorecard (`right`/`missed`/`wrong`/`invented`/`withheld`, never one aggregate), `--evidence` writing `docs/evidence/extraction-provider-scorecard.md` through the pilot's own redaction sweep, and exit code 2 when a named provider has no credential. Default runs are byte-for-byte unchanged, so `make eval-ci` still measures what it measured. | **The routes were not undocumented, they were unreachable, and the two halves needed opposite answers.** The global-DNC surface is an act we can perform TODAY and `dnc-complaint.md` §6 had an operator hand-assembling a step-up header against production while answering a regulator — the exact defect the ops console already closed for the load-shed mode and the dead-letter replay, so it is closed the same way. The WhatsApp routes are stronger still: the API returns `current_notice_text` so a SCREEN can render the exact wording a client is agreeing to, and refuses a stale `notice_version` — a route designed around a console that did not exist. The preference scrub is the honest (b): it records a run performed on an access provider's DLT platform, which needs the Registered Telemarketer relationship R-01 does not yet have, and the console has no campaign identity to hang it on (`/admin` serves no campaign list) — a uuid typed into a form is the curl's failure mode in a nicer font. **Two defects found by building the affordances.** `record_for_client` was WRITE-ONLY: no admin-realm read existed (the client GET deliberately answers no subject state to an impersonated session), so an operator could record a month-old onboarding form over last week's withdrawal, on an append-only ledger where the correction is another row and the alerts went out in between. And `GlobalEntryOut.removable` is always `false` — it is `is_removable()`'s answer about CLIENTS — so gating the ops Release control on it, the obvious reading, would have left the only surface that can lift a global suppression unable to. **On the eval side the trap was the opposite of a bug**: `get_extractor()` falls back to the offline heuristic by design, because a post-call pipeline that failed for want of a key would lose a lead — inheriting that in a comparison would print offline output under a `sarvam` heading, in a scorecard feeding a residency decision. Named providers therefore resolve to nothing rather than to something else, and the AI Studio `GEMINI_API_KEY` cannot produce a gemini column at all (D-127 G-1). The committed artefact says in its own body that it cannot move the first post-call extraction to Gemini whatever the numbers say. **Still blocked outside this repo, named**: a Sarvam API key and egress for task #87's credentialed run; R-01 for anything to record in §8; a Meta WABA (D-91) before an alert can be delivered at all, which is why the client screen withholds the grant while `delivery_available` is false and never withholds the withdrawal. |
| D-137 | **A dashboard assist was unmeterable, unbillable and unbounded at once: the metering key excluded it by predicate, the only ledger row it could have written meant something else, and one Calevate-owned key had no ceiling in either direction** | New `usage_events.ref` + unit types `ai_assist_ktok_in`/`_out` + a SECOND partial unique index `ux_usage_events_tenant_unit_ref` on `(tenant_id, unit_type, ref)` where `ref IS NOT NULL AND call_id IS NULL` — disjoint from `ux_usage_events_tenant_call_unit`'s `call_id IS NOT NULL`, so neither shadows the other (migration `e1a7c93d5b02`, whose downgrade REFUSES rather than dropping idempotency keys off an append-only ledger, and which was run down and back up before it shipped). `billing/ai_quota.py` is the whole policy: a rupee ceiling per tenant per IST month by plan tier, displayed as BOTH rupees and an "about N assists" estimate the server derives; `require_ai_assist` is the ONE gate; `record_ai_assist_usage` is the ONE writer, idempotent on `ref` via `ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING` with the index predicate repeated verbatim; overage is ONE `credit_ledger` row, reason `usage`, ref `ai_assist:<YYYY-MM>`, bought only after a person accepts an echoed amount on `POST /v1/billing/ai-quota/extra` (`org:manage`, audited in the same transaction); and `platform_ai_spend` is the brake on our own key that no per-tenant ceiling can see. `/c/<slug>/ai-assist` is the screen and the G-5 dialog. `_tier_totals` now excludes the AI units so our absorbed cost cannot reach a client's spend, their margin panel or a closed month's statement. | **All three exclusions in the old key were real and each alone was fatal, verified against `pg_indexes` and not against the migration that claims them**: `call_id IS NOT NULL` is a PREDICATE, so an assist row is not in that index at all — not merely unprotected by NULL semantics, which matters because the remedies differ (a NULL inside an index is reachable with `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`; a row excluded by predicate can only ever be covered by a different index); the unit list omits `llm_tok_in`, which is anyway a column with no writer and therefore a finding a new writer would erase rather than fix; and `llm_tok_out` already means "one call's LLM leg" at `qty = 1` (`pipeline.py:1201`) because the engine bills legs with no token count. **The unit is `ktok` and that is a money decision, not a naming one** — `unit_cost_paid` is NUMERIC(12,4), so the smallest expressible price is ₹0.0001 per unit of `qty`, and a Gemini-class input token at ~₹0.000008 would have stored as 0.0000: per-token metering would have counted every assist as free and the ceiling would never have moved. **Two things only the build found.** (1) `usage_events` is shared with the call meter, and `_tier_totals` sums EVERY row for the month grouped by `meta.tts_tier` — so an assist landed in the unattributed bucket and reached three client-facing figures including a closed month's `spend_used_inr`, which is Calevate's absorbed cost printed on a client's own spend; the exclusion is spelled negatively, from the one constant, because a positive list of call units would silently drop `number_rental` out of a client's cost. (2) A platform-wide total is UNASKABLE in app code — `usage_events` is FORCE-RLS'd, an untenanted session sees zero rows, and hard rule 1 forbids the admin DB role — so the brake had to be its own untenanted counter, keyed by month rather than a column on `platform_state`, whose single row every dispatch tick reads. **The overage is a fixed BLOCK, once per tenant-month, and the shape is forced by hard rule 4**: a row that grew with usage would be an UPDATE the `calevate_forbid_mutation` trigger refuses, and per-request billing is what G-4 rules out in words — a block is also the only shape whose modal can state a TRUE figure before the click. Its cost is stated on the screen rather than hidden: the unused part is not refunded and does not carry over. **Sabotage, ten breaks, ten distinct signatures**; the one worth recording is that moving `lock_tenant_credits` to AFTER the read it protects still produced ONE ledger row — `find_entry_by_ref` takes the lock itself — so an outcome-only race test was green and the ordering assertion is what caught it. **Not done, and named**: the metering writer and the gate have no production caller until a user-triggered dashboard-AI ENDPOINT exists (PLAN Part 15's surface; D-134 landed the Vertex client and the extractor, not a console assist route), and a MANAGED tenant at the ceiling is refused with their account manager named rather than offered a wallet debit, because a retainer client's invoice is derived and there is no priced AI-overage line to derive — closed by a founder pricing one, not by code. |
| D-138 | **The last outbound path fetching a third party's address with no guard, found by the pass that D-129 made possible** | `copy_recording` vets `source_url` through `egress_guard.assert_public_http_url` and follows redirects BY HAND — `follow_redirects=False`, bounded by `RECORDING_REDIRECT_LIMIT`, re-vetting every hop. `EgressRefusedError` becomes `StorageUnavailableError` so the pipeline records it against the call. | **The URL is the VENDOR's**, off an engine payload or a poller snapshot — not ours, not a tenant's, and the category this repo trusts least everywhere else (hard rule 2 keeps vendor shapes out of typed columns for exactly this reason). It was the ONE remaining path fetching an externally supplied address with no check and `follow_redirects=True`, so a vendor response naming `169.254.169.254` — or merely redirecting to it — would have been fetched by a worker inside our network and written to our bucket. **Vetting only the first URL is not a guard**: the redirect is the whole bypass, which is why `integrations.service.deliver` already set `follow_redirects=False` and why the hop loop re-runs the check rather than trusting httpx to chase a `Location` nobody judged. The bound exists because an unbounded chain is a worker sitting in a redirect loop until its 60s timeout — a job slot held hostage by a third party. Three sabotages, three distinct signatures: vet-only-the-first-hop reddens the redirect test alone (`the redirect target was fetched`); removing the guard reddens two; unbounding the loop reddens the chain test. The assertion that carries the class is `asked == []` — a refusal AFTER the request is not a refusal, because the response body is the thing an attacker wanted. Found by D-135's adversarial pass, which could only look for this because D-129 had established what the doctrine was. |
| D-139 | **`audit_log.ip` recorded our own nginx on every audited route: the fourth field of SEC-COMP §5, honest-looking and wrong 80 times** | All 80 handlers writing `ip=request.client.host if request.client else None` inline now call `core.auth.client_request_ip(request)`, which asks whether the peer is a trusted proxy before believing a header. New `scripts/check_audit_ip.py` in `make guardrails` AND in CI fails on any `request.client` read under `apps/api` outside that one function, with 7 negative controls in `tests/audit_ip_guard_test.py`. | **D-131 fixed the definition and left the callers, and said so** — its docstring carried the grep as a worklist and the reason it must not read as a design, because a 30-file read-modify-write against five concurrent agents is how a previous session lost every tracked edit. This is that worklist closed. **The count was the finding**: the audit that produced the plan named ONE call site (the impersonation dependency); the real number was 80 across 26 files, so the compliance record was defective platform-wide rather than at one dependency. **The guard is the deliverable, not the sweep.** A worklist in a docstring is a promise kept by whoever reads it; a check is a guarantee, and the next author reaching for `request.client` is now stopped by the build rather than by whether a reviewer remembered a paragraph. It bans the PEER READ rather than the `ip=` spelling, because the next author will spell it differently and what is actually wrong is consulting the peer instead of the predicate. **The allowance is bound to the FUNCTION, not the file** — a control proves that moving the read one function down in `core/auth.py` is still refused, which would otherwise have made that file a hole — and it **fails if `client_request_ip` stops reading the peer at all**, because a green check over an absent guarantee is worse than a red one. AST rather than grep, so the prose in `core/auth.py` explaining this very rule is not a finding (the lesson `check_model_residency` learned about its own docstring). `apps/voice-runtime` is out of scope deliberately: it reads the peer to decide whether the peer is trusted, which is the same argument-not-answer distinction the allowance rests on. |
| D-141 | **The missing-existence-check class was not exhausted: two more 500s, a refusal about the wrong thing, three cheerful 200s, and the one destructive step-up in the repo that named no subject** | `tests/absent_tenant_answer_test.py` is the CENSUS rather than a fifth one-off: every route the app serves under `/v1/admin/tenants/{tenant_id}` and `/v1/ops/tenants/{tenant_id}` is driven with an id no organization holds and must answer 404 `not_found`, with a completeness assertion that fails on a route absent from its own table and a staleness assertion on the one exemption. Seven fixed. `POST …/dlt-registration` and `POST …/first-campaign-review` answered **500 `internal_error`** off their tenant FKs; `GET`/`POST …/whatsapp-alerts` answered **422 `alert_optin_no_owner_with_a_number`** ("add a mobile number to the owner's profile"); `GET …/margin`, `GET …/commercial-terms` and `GET …/invitations` answered **200** with a ₹0 margin card, `state: none` and `[]`; `GET …/agents/{agent_id}/prompt` answered **200 `[]`**, closed in `prompts.list_prompt_versions` through the same `_agent_state` the two writes beside it use. `DELETE /v1/ops/dnc/global/{entry_id}` gains `release_globally_confirmation(entry_id)` — runbook §6, `opsDnc.ts` and both suites moved in the same change. `route_shape_test` grows the CONVERSE of D-130's rule (nothing in the admin path space may admit a client principal), and `agent_voice_test`'s `in (401, 403)` becomes an exact answer with a positive control. | **D-133 fixed four instances of a class and the class kept producing**, because the fix was per route: `record_dlt_registration`'s own docstring says it is "the same family, same permission and same shape" as the two that got the guard, and it was missed for a reason worth naming — it is an `ON CONFLICT` upsert, which reads like a write that cannot fail on a key. The whatsapp pair is D-133's `number_taken` defect in another module: a real refusal, correct for the state it was written for, sending an operator to edit a profile in an account that is not there. **The three 200s are the worst of the seven and the reason the census had to be a census**: none of them is an empty payload anyone would notice — a ₹0 margin, "no terms have ever been set" and "no invitation is outstanding" are the payloads a REAL new client produces, and each sat beside a POST on the same screen that already answered 404 for the same id. **On the step-up**: `core/stepup.py` says the header exists so "the confirmation captured for one action cannot be replayed against another", and every subject-bearing string in the repo binds its subject — except this one, whose recorded reason (hard rule 6 forbids a phone number in a header) is sound for the sibling POST, whose subject is a list of numbers, and does not apply to a DELETE whose subject is a uuid already in the path. It is the act that re-permits dialling somebody who asked not to be dialled, and the runbook printed a curl an operator could re-run against a different id. **On D-130's weakened voice test, measured rather than argued**: downgrading `VoiceSetter` to `realm="client"` leaves the behavioural test GREEN, because the handler also takes `AdminSession` and `core.deps.admin_db` depends on `current_admin` — a real second lock, but one that means no request can distinguish the declaration from the session dependency, so the declaration is now asserted off the route table (71 routes, all clean today). **On D-132's branch dominance, re-measured**: removing the per-query gate gives **43 hits**, several of which are the LADDER ITSELF (`campaign-review:152`, `prompt:939`, `[tenantId]:554`) — an ungated rule would flag its own remedy, and the correct spelling here renders the refusal as a SIBLING of the data branch, so there is no dominance to compute. Left open, with the number written into the guard. Ten sabotages, ten distinct signatures. |
| D-142 | **The one vendor fact D-127 rests on got re-searched and the answer moved the wrong way: `asia-south1` is very likely NOT serving the model we ship, and four things that were true-ish became true** | **The gate first.** `calevate_shared.engine.GEMINI_MODEL_CONFIRMED_IN_REGION: Final = False` is minted beside `GEMINI_DEFAULT_LLM` — a ninth capability constant, so the eight prose sites claiming Gemini 3.x runs in Mumbai now bind something `check_docs_drift` §5 can fail on. It is promoted from a footnote in OPERATIONS §2's D-31 blockquote to **numbered hard gate 14** with its own pass/fail and its own wrong-fix warning, restated in DEV-SETUP §1 where a person reads BEFORE they spend an hour in the GCP console, and re-scoped in BRD R-04. `VertexGeminiExtractor._log_refusal` makes the 404 answer itself: `vertex_model_not_served_in_region` carries region, model, project, the flag READ (not quoted) and the remedy, and every other non-2xx logs `vertex_request_refused` with the STATUS — the whole diagnosis used to reach the log as the word `HTTPStatusError`, with 401/403/404/429/503 indistinguishable. The body is still never logged (Google's error bodies quote the request, and the request is a transcript). **Three more seams closed in the same pass.** (1) `google_oauth`'s bearer cache is keyed `(client_email, private_key_id, scope)` and its deadline moved to `time.monotonic()`; the superseded slot is dropped on the replacement's first successful mint, so a rotation cannot grow the dict. `platform_config`'s `AppliesRule(LIVE)` on `gcp_service_account_json` loses its caveat paragraph because the caveat is gone. (2) `build_vertex_response_schema` de-duplicates `propertyOrdering`. (3) `run_assist` takes `quota_exhausted` and returns `AssistResult.usage` (`TokenUsage`, thinking tokens folded into output), which are D-137's two missing wire-ends. `check_model_residency`'s self-exemption shrinks from the whole file to `_host_definition` — a template that IS one of the two banned host strings. New `tests/google_oauth_test.py` (11), plus 11 in `vertex_extraction_test` and 3 in `model_residency_guard_test`. | **The re-search did not merely fail to confirm; it pointed the other way, and that is a different fact.** D-134 recorded `asia-south1` serving `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` as unverified because the docs are egress-blocked. Searching again on 16 Aug 2026 (`docs.cloud.google.com`, `firebase.google.com`, `discuss.google.dev`, promptfoo, litellm and modelavailability.com are ALL still refused, so this is REPORTED, NOT READ) produced a consistent picture that nobody had assembled: independent summaries place the 3.x family on the GLOBAL endpoint and the `us`/`eu` multi-region REP endpoints, one reports it absent even from `us-central1`, Google documents that "some models are only available in the global region, especially on initial release" — and what search DOES place in Mumbai is the 2.5 Flash / 2.5 Flash-Lite class, *the family BRD R-04 retires on 16 Oct 2026*. So the gate has a calendar on it and R-04 is no longer neutralised for the dashboard leg. **What survived, and it is the leg that matters**: `asia-south1` IS in Vertex's own ML-processing-location table with Tokyo, Singapore, Sydney and Seoul, so the REGION's residency property stands and only the MODEL is in doubt — which is why the wrong fix (`locations/global`, on which Google says the caller cannot control or know where processing happens) is named in the gate, in the 404's log line and in SEC-COMP §4 rather than left for somebody to discover at 3am. **Two exemptions were checked rather than assumed, and they answered differently.** The residency guard's docstring filter was DEAD when Part 12 shipped — its author sabotaged it and the CLI stayed green — and D-134's own Vertex client made it live: `extraction.py:292` explains the AI Studio ban by naming the host, so disabling the filter now reports that explanation as the offence, and a test pins exactly that. The self-skip was a REAL hole and is now two literals: the guard was the only module in `apps/`, `packages/` and `scripts/` where a `us-central1` URL passed, which is a poor place for the single hole because a guardrail is edited by whoever is relaxing it. **`gemini_api_key` is not mid-deprecation and never was**, which its own comment said twice in contradictory directions ("stop reading it (done — nothing in the tree does)" three lines above "it is read in exactly one place"). A field that is READ has not had hard rule 8's step one taken, so there is no step two to schedule and the sentence promising a later release was a schedule wearing a rule's clothes. It is kept permanently: it is the only input separating "no AI credential" from "the WRONG KIND of AI credential", and deleting it would send an operator who did install a Gemini key to go and check their typing. **Two tests were caught passing for the wrong reason before they shipped.** `httpx.MockTransport` with a sync handler never suspends, so four `gather`ed token requests ran strictly in sequence and the race test observed one exchange — it would have observed one against a cache with no concurrency safety at all; one `await asyncio.sleep(0)` in the fake is the difference. And a "no vendor body in the log" assertion greping for the words `invalid`/`message` matched `LogRecord`'s own machinery, so the fake now returns a distinctive marker. The race itself is recorded rather than fixed: N cold callers mint N valid tokens, the cache converges, and a per-key `asyncio.Lock` in a module-level dict costs a lock created on one event loop and awaited on another. A 401 mid-flight deliberately does NOT evict — the margin means a bearer is never handed over older than 55 minutes, so a 401 is a statement about the KEY, and with the key id in the cache key installing a new one now self-heals on the next call. **Not done, and it is one line in another agent's file.** `llm_tok_in` — a `usage_events` CHECK value with no writer, ever — is still there. D-134 deferred it on a migration collision that is now OVER (`e1a7c93d5b02` has landed, so a new revision is a child and not a conflict). The removal is exactly: delete the string from `CLIENT_BILLED_UNIT_TYPES` in `apps/api/billing/models.py` and rewrite the paragraph above it, plus one child migration that rebuilds `ck_usage_events_unit_type_enum` without it, freezing its unit list as literals at that revision the way `b8d3f47c2a19` and `e1a7c93d5b02` both do. `apps/api/billing/models.py` belongs to another agent in this wave, and the constant and the constraint cannot move separately — `tests/ai_quota_test.py` holds them equal — so it is reported here rather than half-applied. |
| D-143 | **The guard was vetting a name the transport was never going to use — two IDNA standards read one URL as two hosts, with no race to win — and three more things a third party was choosing for us** | `egress_guard.assert_public_http_url` now ends by asking the TRANSPORT where it would go: `httpx.URL`'s parse of the same string must name the same host bytes and the same effective port, or the destination is refused as `webhook_url_ambiguous` (published in WEBHOOKS.md §1.6). A URL httpx will not parse at all is refused by the same clause, which also stops `httpx.InvalidURL` — NOT an `httpx.HTTPError`, so no caller catches it — escaping as an unhandled exception. `UnicodeError` joins the fail-closed resolver clause. `VettedDestination` gains `url`, and `deliver` and `copy_recording` request THAT rather than their own argument, so what was judged and what is sent cannot drift. `copy_recording` streams with `MAX_RECORDING_BYTES` (= `CALL_CAP_MAX_S` x 32 kB/s, enforced on the running total, with a `Content-Length` pre-flight) inside `RECORDING_FETCH_DEADLINE_S = 120s` over the WHOLE fetch. `bootstrap._ops_detail_gate` short-circuits on a missing credential and admits at most `_DETAIL_GATE_CONCURRENCY = 2` authorisations at once, logging `health_detail_gate_saturated`. `integration_endpoint.created`'s `events` is joined, not a list. 22 new tests across the three suites. | **The parser differential is not the TOCTOU window D-129 weighed pinning against — it is worse, because it needs no race.** `socket.getaddrinfo` IDNA-encodes a `str` host with the STDLIB codec (`Modules/socketmodule.c::idna_converter`, IDNA 2003); httpx uses the `idna` package (IDNA 2008 / UTS-46). Measured on this interpreter: `fass.example.com` is what our lookup vets, `xn--fa-hia.example.com` is what httpx connects to. Owning one name and pointing the other at `127.0.0.1` is a deterministic SSRF, and re-judging pinning is what surfaced it. Pinning STAYS rejected — the residual is now a test (`the caller connects by name and never by `VettedDestination.addresses``) rather than only a paragraph. **The full outbound enumeration D-135 asked for, done**: 18 egress sites in `apps/`+`packages/`. Exactly TWO fetch an externally supplied address — the tenant webhook (D-129) and the vendor recording (D-138) — and its claim that `copy_recording` was the last unguarded one is CONFIRMED. Eleven are hardcoded vendor constants (`api.bolna.ai`, `api.cartesia.ai`, `api.razorpay.com`, `api.clerk.com`, `graph.facebook.com` x2, `sheets.googleapis.com`, `oauth2.googleapis.com`, `api.sarvam.ai`, the three `secret_probes` URLs); Bolna's pagination `next` link is already pinned to its own base host; the Sheets path interpolates a tenant id that `parse_spreadsheet_ref` re-validates on READ as well as on write; `apps/voice-runtime` makes NO outbound HTTP call at all. The remaining five (S3 endpoint, SMTP, OTLP, Redis, JWKS host) are operator configuration behind `platform:config`/`platform:secrets`, a different trust class, and are named rather than guarded. **D-128 put an authentication attempt on the two routes carrying `PROFILES["exempt"]`**, and D-135's own `_signing_key_for` docstring names the consequence — an unknown `kid` forces an un-memoised JWKS refetch, "and on `/healthz*` there is not even a rate-limit profile in front of it". That fix moved the fetch onto a `min(32, cpu+4)` thread pool shared with the whole process; moving a hazard to a scarcer resource is not bounding it, and the MOUNTING was the half left open. **Re-judged rather than inherited**: the `[phone]` masking of a Drive id stands (roughly one registration in a million, and `object_id` plus `outbound_webhooks.url` hold it in full), but `events` was collapsing to `"[1 items]"` on 100% of rows — the same trap `core.health` had already dodged in the same wave, so the audit line for "who pointed our leads outward, and where" recorded the COUNT of what started flowing. **Checked and found solid**: CI does run MinIO and `exit 1`s if it is unhealthy, so the 3 skips are local-only; all 174 routes but 10 declare a permission and every `/v1/ops/*` is `ops:manage`/`platform:*`; the 15s delivery worst case sits under a 300s `job_timeout` with no lease or tick assuming otherwise; the conftest resolver double is unreachable from `apps/` (now a test). **Two of my own tests were caught passing for the wrong reason before they shipped**: a 64-char-label test that never reached `getaddrinfo` because the conftest double answers `.example` without it, and a byte-cap test that went GREEN with the cap deleted because the `Content-Length` clause refused first — the body is undeclared now, and the two messages are distinct. Twelve sabotages, twelve distinct signatures. |
| D-140 | **The dashboard-AI money path was four unfalsifiable constants, a metering key a browser could have chosen, two clocks disagreeing about which month a rupee belongs to, and a brake that only announced itself by refusing somebody** | The prices are now ONE sourced table (`ASSIST_LIST_PRICE_INR_PER_KTOK` — `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` at $0.25/$1.50 per 1M, ₹95.66/USD) and `AI_ASSIST_NOMINAL_INR` is DERIVED from it times a stated reference assist and margin; it evaluates to the ₹0.50 that was typed, so the value is unchanged and now checkable, and `tests/ai_quota_test.py` holds all four product terms inside bands that fail on a misplaced decimal point. `record_ai_assist_usage` accepts only `new_assist_ref()`'s `assist:<uuid7>` and logs a replay at WARNING instead of returning silently. The platform counter is bumped for the month the ROW landed in, read back from the insert's `RETURNING`, never the API process's clock. `purchase_ai_overage` refuses inside `LAST_SALEABLE_MINUTES` of the IST month (`ai_extra_month_ending`, new `plans.ist_month_end`, which `month_pricing_instant` now shares) and its three hand-written refusals collapse into the ONE `EXTRA_REFUSAL` table that `extra_unavailable` and `require_ai_assist` also read. `record_ai_assist_usage` announces 80% and 100% of the brake through `alert()` on the bump that CROSSES the line. `UNIT_TYPES` is now derived from `CLIENT_BILLED_UNIT_TYPES` + `PLATFORM_ABSORBED_UNIT_TYPES`. `FieldScore.record`'s bare `getattr` is documented as load-bearing and pinned. | **The metering key was an off switch pointed at ourselves.** `ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING` means a `ref` seen before meters nothing, moves no quota and moves no platform counter — and `billing/models.py` invited a browser to supply it ("the caller mints one `ref` per request and re-sends it"), so one constant string would have bought unlimited assists on Calevate's credential, past a ceiling that could no longer move, invisible to the brake. Double-click protection deduped AFTER the provider was paid, which hides the spend rather than saving it. **Two clocks, and the halves never reconcile**: `occurred_at` is Postgres's `now()` and the counter was keyed by the API process's `datetime.now(UTC)`, so ordinary NTP skew at an IST boundary guards a month that spent nothing while the month that spent it goes unguarded. **The 23:59 sale is two bugs in one**: a block that expires in seconds, and a roll between `read_ai_quota` and `record_entry` that debits ₹500 under a key next month's read does not look for — the client is down the money, holds no allowance, and is offered the block again; an hour-wide refusal makes the interval unreachable where a re-read would only have detected it. **The order the docstring claimed was already wrong**: `extra_unavailable` put `not_prepaid` ahead of `already_purchased`, so a tenant moved to `managed` after buying was told their plan cannot buy a thing they had already bought. **The brake's own release note was true and insufficient** — a `transient` ProblemError really is a 503 and really does fire `alert()` (verified in `core/errors.install_error_handlers`), but only when a client is refused, and nothing anywhere read `platform_ai_spend`, so "how close are we" needed psql. **The order-of-magnitude check found the numbers sound and the prose stale**: the ktok argument was computed at ~₹0.000008 a token when the real figure is ₹0.0000239, and the claim that a per-token price stores as zero is true of input and NOT of output, which rounds to ₹0.0001 — not zero, and still 30% of the price discarded, which is the case a reader would have assumed was fine. **Fourteen sabotages, fourteen distinct signatures, and one of them exposed a test of mine that passed for the wrong reason**: `record()`'s guard is `getattr`, not `slots=True`, so the `slots` I had added earned nothing and its rationale was false — reverted, and the test now fails on the `getattr(self, verdict, 0)` edit that would actually break it. **NOT CLOSED, and this is the one that matters**: the metering writer and the gate still have no production caller. An assist endpoint cannot meter honestly because `Extractor.run()` returns the parsed JSON and DISCARDS `usageMetadata`/`usage`, so no token count exists anywhere outside `apps/workers/extraction.py`; writing an estimate into a column named `ai_assist_ktok_*` would be worse than no caller. The join is one field on `AssistResult` away and it belongs to whoever owns that file. |

resolution was not only a cost: with the Redis dedupe marker unavailable, `_record_impersonated_read` fails towards recording — correctly — and one impersonated request therefore wrote TWO identical overrides, computed by removing the rule and re-resolving. **The same blindness in the CORS suite**: every assertion there checks that what we serve is ALLOWED, and `*` allows everything — Starlette pairs it with `allow_credentials=True` by echoing the caller's Origin, and `bootstrap.py`'s "a wildcard is never acceptable here" was a comment with nothing behind it. **§3's "echo on response" held for every response except the three the stack produces itself**: the 413, 429 and 503 carried no `X-Correlation-Id` even when the caller sent one, `trace_id: null`, and no `request` log line — the three refusals support is most often asked about were the three with nothing to trace them by. **D-139's guard knew one spelling of the peer read while a second was already in the tree it was guarding** (`core/middleware.py` reads `scope.get("client")`), which defeats the stated rationale that the next author will spell it differently; `apps/workers` stays out of scope, now verified rather than assumed — it has no `Request`, no scope and no `write_audit` call. **The signup quota's "not a directory of who signed up from where" was contradicted one module over**: an unkeyed digest of an IPv4 address encodes a 32-bit space, the limiter writes the same address in the clear, and SEC-COMP §5 keeps it permanently in `audit_log.ip` — so `fingerprint` is now for the one live credential and a test pins its single caller. **The suite was measuring its neighbours**: `ASGITransport` peers at `127.0.0.1`, so ~4,000 tests and every concurrent run shared one bucket against a `bulk_read` ceiling of six — 53 failures became 1, all of them 429s that passed standalone, verified over eight consecutive runs in 27s and against a heavy suite running concurrently. **Still open, in another batch's files**: `test_the_last_owner_rule_survives_two_owners_demoting_each_other` is timing-flaky under load independently of this change (interleaved A/B: 5/8 failures before, 7/8 after) — the loser's principal can resolve after the winner commits, which is a legitimate 403 the test does not admit. |
| D-145 | **A test that read a file anyone could overwrite, and a model chosen because the region would not move** | **The ratchet's parser test stopped reading `REPO_ROOT/.coverage`.** `TestReportParsing` now reads the `real_run` fixture's own scratch measurement — a genuine `coverage run` this file already builds for `TestRefusalReachesTheExitCode`, so the property the test existed for (parse REAL coverage.py output, not a fixture that can drift) is kept at zero extra cost, and its `pytest.skip` on a fresh checkout is gone with it. `load_report`, `manifest_path`, `unvouched_run` and `blind_spots` lose their `data_file=None` defaults, so the well-known path is now reachable from exactly two places: `main()`'s argparse default (which run is scored) and the plugin's uninstrumented-run fallback (which stale file is invalidated). Two new controls: `test_a_partial_coverage_at_the_repo_root_cannot_fail_it` plants a REAL one-module `coverage run` over the live `.coverage` and restores it byte- and mtime-for-mtime, and `test_no_reader_defaults_to_the_repo_root_data_file` pins the signatures. **And the dashboard AI ships on `gemini-2.5-flash`.** `GEMINI_DEFAULT_LLM` moves off `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`; `gemini-2.5-flash` leaves `GEMINI_RETIRED_LLMS` (it cannot be both banned and sent) and both jobs that entry was doing land elsewhere rather than being dropped — the literal ban becomes `test_no_shipped_module_spells_the_gemini_default`, and the DATE becomes `GEMINI_DEFAULT_LLM_RETIRES: Final = date(2026, 10, 16)` plus `test_the_shipped_gemini_model_has_runway_left`, which turns CI red on 16 Sep 2026 with the remedy in the failure message. OPERATIONS §2 gate 14 is rewritten and gains **dated gate 14b** (replace the identifier before 16 Oct 2026, three steps, three wrong answers); BRD R-04 stops being a risk that was routed around and becomes an obligation that is owed; CLAUDE.md, AGENTS, README, TRD, SEC-COMP §4, DEV-SETUP and BUILD-LOG follow. the residency flag was deliberately NOT flipped. | **The coverage coupling had a mechanism, and it is worth knowing because it will outlive this fix**: `coverage`'s source walk skips any directory without an `__init__.py`, and D-18 makes `apps/voice-runtime` hyphenated, so a partial run's data file contains no line of hard rule 3's service — `voice-runtime-ack` matches zero files and the assertion that fires is `every area must match measured files`, on a surface the diff never touched. Reproduced deliberately before fixing anything: one `coverage run -m pytest <one file>` at the repo root, and the test went red. **Two other options were argued and rejected.** `COVERAGE_FILE` redirection moves the collision rather than removing it and costs three commands plus CI; teaching the TEST to recognise a whole-suite run is the gate's job and the gate already does it far better (`unvouched_run` pairs the data file with a manifest) — a test cannot, because inside the suite the newest manifest describes the PREVIOUS run, so the honest version would skip exactly when a partial file was present. **Planting the poison in the real tree was measured, not assumed**: `coverage run` erases its data file at start and holds no handle until the save at exit, so a file written and removed mid-suite cannot reach the gate's own measurement. **On the model, the search moved the other way from D-142 and the flag still did not.** Independent summaries now place the 2.5 class in Mumbai with ML processing (2.5 Flash, 2.5 Pro, 2.5 Flash-Lite) and still place no 3.x model there; a Google developer-forum thread is titled asking for anything in `asia-south1` more capable THAN Gemini 2.5 Flash. That is the good news D-142 lacked — and it is the same class of evidence D-142 refused, so the residency flag was left where D-142 put it: `docs.cloud.google.com`, `discuss.ai.google.dev`, `modelavailability.com`, `innfactory.ai`, `gcloud-compute.com`, `openrouter.ai` and `pricepertoken.com` were each ATTEMPTED from here and each returned EGRESS_BLOCKED, so this is REPORTED, NOT READ, and one 200 from `generateContent` is what closes it — blocked on a GCP project and nothing else. **The founder's trade, stated: a live 16 Oct 2026 deadline bought in exchange for a model the only permitted region is reported to serve**, because D-127 does not move the region and a 404 is not better than a deadline. The runway is 30 days rather than more for a reason worth recording — the decision landed 61 days out, so any runway of 61+ is red on arrival, and a gate that is red on arrival is one somebody deletes. **Not done, and it is another agent's file.** `apps/api/billing/ai_quota.py` still prices the assist at 3.1 Flash-Lite. The exact change is `ASSIST_LIST_PRICE_INR_PER_KTOK` `in` 0.0239 → **0.0287** and `out` 0.1435 → **0.2392** (2.5 Flash at $0.30/$2.50 per 1M, ₹95.66/USD), which carries `reference_assist_cost_inr()` 0.33475 → 0.50230 and `AI_ASSIST_NOMINAL_INR` ₹0.50 → ₹0.75 by derivation. The PRICE is the one fact here that was genuinely READ, not reported: LiteLLM's `model_prices_and_context_window.json` on `raw.githubusercontent.com` (unblocked) gives `gemini-2.5-flash` under `vertex_ai-language-models` as 3e-07 in and 2.5e-06 out, and gives 3.1 Flash-Lite the $0.25/$1.50 the current table was built from, so it is the same source of truth. **Two of that file's own bands then fail, and one of them is a product finding rather than a test to edit**: the output leg loses only 16.4% to per-token rounding against an assertion of 25% (the claim that this ledger must count in thousands still holds — input loses 100% — so the threshold moves and its comment is rewritten with both real numbers), and a ₹25 trial allowance now buys **49.8** assists against a floor of 50, i.e. at 2.5 Flash prices the trial tier no longer clears its own enough-to-form-an-opinion bar. That is `AI_QUOTA_INR['trial']`, a price, and a founder's to move. **Eight sabotages, eight distinct signatures**, including the one that proves the doc binding is live: flipping the flag to True without touching prose fails `check_docs_drift` §5 on all TEN sentences at once, so the day gate 14 answers 200 they move together or not at all. |
| D-146 | **The seam: a dashboard assist a user can actually reach, and the price table caught up with the model it meters** | **PART 1 — the join.** `POST /v1/calls/{call_id}/assist` (`org:manage`, client realm, `costly` limiter) is the production caller D-137 and D-140 both named as the thing that did not exist: SUBJECT → GATE → RUN → METER in one handler, with each arrow argued in `apps/api/crm/assist.py`. New `crm/assist.py` owns the two halves that are not a route: `load_assist_source` builds the model's input from `transcript_turns.text_redacted` — the RAW column is not a parameter of its query and is not named in the file — and `meter_assist` turns `AssistResult.usage` into `record_ai_assist_usage`'s two `ai_assist_ktok_*` rows at `ASSIST_LIST_PRICE_INR_PER_KTOK`. `Idempotency-Key` is REQUIRED (400 + problem body, draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header-07 §2.4) because a repeat is a second payment to Google and D-140 moved dedupe here specifically so it happens BEFORE the provider is paid. `CallAssistOut` is three required fields — `summary` (through `crm.service.redacted_summary`, entered in `check_redaction_exposure.KNOWN_SAFE_FIELDS` and its pin), `disclosure`, `metered` — and NOTHING is persisted. The call detail screen grows `AssistCard`; `AcceptChargeDialog` and `extraUnavailableSentence` MOVE out of `c/[slug]/ai-assist/page.tsx` into `components/aiExtraDialog.tsx` with that page migrated in the same change, so the ceiling opens the ONE wallet dialog from both entry points. `tests/call_assist_test.py` (20) + `apps/web/tests/callAssist.test.tsx` (10). **PART 2 — the price.** `ASSIST_LIST_PRICE_INR_PER_KTOK` moves to `gemini-2.5-flash`'s $0.30/$2.50 per 1M (₹0.0287/₹0.2392 per ktok), carrying `reference_assist_cost_inr()` ₹0.33475 → ₹0.50230 and `AI_ASSIST_NOMINAL_INR` ₹0.50 → ₹0.75 by derivation; `AI_QUOTA_INR['trial']` ₹25 → ₹40; `billing/models.py`'s ktok paragraph and three worked examples in `ai_quota.py` recomputed. | **The two halves were one call apart and neither could be exercised, which is why both delivering agents put the same line at the top of their report.** `run_assist`'s redaction guard — the one thing standing between this product and D-127 G-2 — was DEAD: nothing called the function, so the branch that refuses unredacted input had never run against anything but a test. This route is what makes it live, and the sabotage that matters is the one that proves it: swapping `text_redacted` for `text` in one SQL string turns TEN tests red, and the FIRST of them is not the assertion about bytes — it is `run_assist` refusing its own caller with `assist_input_not_redacted`. The guard catches the mistake the route was most likely to make, from inside, exactly as its author predicted. **`AssistResult.usage is None` was the decision this join had to make and it has two causes that must not meter the same.** A Sarvam fallback is free (D-36) and writes nothing; a GEMINI answer Vertex did not count is money we spent and cannot account for, so it writes nothing AND fires `alert("CORE_LOGIC", "ai_assist_unmeterable")` — the per-tenant ceiling and the platform brake are both blind to that spend, and an operator can act on "the meter has stopped" where they cannot act on a plausible invented number. Metering it as `qty = 0` was the tempting third answer and is D-140's own objection with the sign flipped: an assist that certainly burned thousands of tokens recorded as zero thousand is a fabricated quantity, and it would ALSO move `requests_used` (`COUNT(DISTINCT ref)`), so the rupee ceiling that blocks and the request count a person plans around would start disagreeing about the same month. **Three departures are argued rather than assumed.** (1) The model is called from a REQUEST HANDLER, which CLAUDE.md forbids — the rule protects the latency-critical path and a blocking worker, and this is neither (every hop is awaited on an asyncio loop); what it really costs is one pooled Postgres connection held across the vendor round trip, because `app.tenant_id` is transaction-local so the transaction cannot be committed and resumed. Bounded by `EXTRACTION_TIMEOUT_S` and the `costly` profile, and taken because the alternative — 202-and-poll — needs somewhere to PUT the answer, which is a new store of transcript-derived prose (D-126's class) that erasure and retention would then have to enumerate. (2) The assist writes NOTHING to `calls` or `call_extractions`: the stored record is the raw-transcript pass, which is the only one that can capture a callback number, and overwriting it with a redacted-pass reading would degrade the lead and rewrite history a client may have exported. (3) `org:manage`, because a POST that spends money must be in `MUTATING_PERMISSIONS` (D-22) and the whole AI surface is already owner-scoped — `billing:read` to see the allowance, `org:manage` to buy more. A new `calls:assist` was rejected on `bulk_leads`'s ground: its holders would be exactly `leads:write`'s. **`quota_exhausted=quota.at_ceiling` is a wire that carries a constant today and says so**: `require_ai_assist` RAISES at the ceiling rather than answering "no", which is G-5's block, so the parameter is False on every path that reaches it — written as the READ rather than as a literal `False`, which would be a promise about another module's control flow made in this one. **On the price, the evidence standing is better than anything else Google-shaped in this repo and that is the finding**: the LiteLLM price file was FETCHED from `raw.githubusercontent.com` and PARSED LOCALLY (3e-07 / 2.5e-06 for `gemini-2.5-flash` under `vertex_ai-language-models`), because an earlier fetch SUMMARY of the same 1.7 MB document returned a different vendor's Flash-Lite row and would have under-priced every assist eightfold — a summariser answering about the wrong row is indistinguishable from an answer. **A 50% price move in one model decision then broke two bands, and only one of them was a test's fault.** The output leg now loses 16.4% to per-token rounding against a 25% floor: the ktok argument SURVIVES (input still loses 100%, so half of every assist would meter free) but it is getting thinner as output prices rise, so the threshold moved to 15%, the input leg is pinned separately as the load-bearing half, and the comment says the next drop is a re-argument and not another lowering. The other was real: ₹25 bought 49.77 assists against a floor of 50, so at 2.5 Flash prices the trial tier no longer cleared its own "enough to form an opinion" bar — the test was right and the constant was wrong. ₹40 buys 79.6 (1.6× the floor) and stays above it until an assist costs ₹0.80, a 59% rise — more than the 50% just absorbed — which matters because two more moves are dated (16 Oct 2026 retirement, 1 Jan 2027 price cliff) and a value tuned to clear 50.0 exactly is one revision from being back here. What a trial tenant SEES is 53, not 79.6, because `requests_included` divides by the margin-loaded nominal; both clear 50, which is what makes the screen and the gate agree. **Nine sabotages, nine distinct signatures**, including one that only the frontend suite could catch (deleting the disclosure block leaves every backend assertion green) and one that shows the fixture rot this change exposed: `aiQuota.test.tsx` claimed 200 assists for a ₹100 allowance, which no server can now answer, so three fixtures were recomputed. **NOT DONE, and it is one line in another agent's file.** `apps/workers/extraction.py`'s `ASSIST_QUOTA_ENFORCED: Final = False` is now FALSE-in-the-code and true-in-the-world: its own comment says "no route calls `require_ai_assist`, hands its verdict to `run_assist`, and meters `AssistResult.usage` back" and names this endpoint as what closes it. One route now does all three. Flipping it is `extraction.py:978` plus `tests/vertex_extraction_test.py:412`, both owned by the agent editing that file in this wave, and a greppable honesty boolean is exactly the thing two agents must not edit at once. |
| D-147 | **The receiver measured two waits it did not bound, went silent on the two exits it did not measure, and cached duplicates by a key that let anyone bypass the cache** | `apps/voice-runtime` gains bounds and accounting where it had measurement and prose. **`_BODY_DEADLINE_S = 2.0`** wraps `_read_bounded`: the comment above `_DURABLE_DEADLINE_S` claimed Postgres was the only unbounded wait on the ack path and the body read was the other one — a trickled body was answered **202 with `X-Ack-Ms: 1506.1`**, correctly measured, correctly alerted, bounded by nothing. `ClientDisconnect` out of `request.stream()` is caught and answered 400 `body_incomplete`. **`measured()`** funnels every non-ack exit through the same header and metric: the inbox's 409 and any 5xx shipped with NO `X-Ack-Ms` and no `webhook_ack_ms` sample at all. The Redis fast-path key becomes the unit of work `{engine}:{execution_id}:{raw_status}` — the same transition the inbox claims — with **`_body_digest(raw)` as its VALUE**, so a replay whose bytes differ is absorbed AND counted (`record_webhook_replay_divergence`). `_ack`/`_refuse`/`_read_bounded` take an **`AckMeter`**, so the in-call tool endpoint records `tool_ack_ms` instead of diluting the receiver's series (closes `tool_endpoint_budget_test`'s `KNOWN_OPEN_ACK_METRICS`). `engine_intake` tries each of the three execution-id spellings instead of `a or b or c` then one type check, and `_keyable` strips surrounding whitespace. New `tests/voice_runtime_deploy_independence_test.py`; `voice_runtime_import_surface_test` drives the tool endpoint and every error branch, and pins what the ALERT DELIVERY THREAD acquires. | **The old body-in-the-key was not a control, and that was verified rather than argued.** A doctored re-delivery driven against the pristine receiver returned 202 `duplicate`, ZERO alerts, an unchanged `payload_hash` and three Postgres statements: the inbox is handed `body_hash({engine, execution_id, raw_status})`, a pure function of the key, so its "same key, different hash ⇒ doctored replay" branch is a tautology at this endpoint and cannot fire on a body change (deliberate, D-40 era). All the old key bought was routing that delivery to a database with nothing to say — 15 Postgres statements for five replays carrying one varying integer, i.e. a round-trip amplifier for anyone past the source check — and ~6.4ms of CPU at the megabyte cap re-serialising the payload to hash it, on the one event loop D-55 puts at ~250 acks/s. Hashing the raw bytes costs ~0.64ms and now DETECTS the divergence the old shape only paid for. **`ClientDisconnect` was an availability hole, not a cosmetic 500**: it fired `alert()` under `ROUTE_HANDLER:unhandled_exception`, and `_admit` suppresses a fingerprint for 15 minutes — one half-open POST per quarter hour kept the receiver's real crash alarm permanently silent. **`proxy_request_buffering off` on the `hooks.` vhost is why the edge does not cover the body read**: nginx streams, and `client_body_timeout` bounds the gap between reads, not the total. **Re-measured on a shared box, indicative not deterministic**: receiver 4.5ms p50 single-flight, 881ms p50 at 250 in flight (budget crossed near ~140, flat distribution = D-55's convoy); tool endpoint 0.6ms p50 single-flight and 103ms p50 at 175 concurrent, confirming D-109's ~175 estimate; a 1000-wide refusal storm costs 0.1ms p50 and zero round trips. Deploy independence proven by serving from a database 48 migrations behind head. **Still open and now visible instead of invisible**: `alerting._deliver` imports `apps.workers.transport` into this process on first alert delivery, which D-49 recorded as kept clean by being lazy — deferring it moved it out of the boot graph, not out of the process. |
| D-148 | **The post-call pipeline's own re-drive could be blocked by the vendor, and two of the ingest job's five steps had no failure policy at all** | `ingest_engine_event` now wraps every stage in the ONE policy `_abandon_ingest` owns (payload parse included, as a `validation` ProblemError mirroring `_post_call_target`), instead of only the engine fetch and the enqueue; the recording copy is skipped when `calls.recording_url` already holds our key (`_copy_recording_once`, with its own stage span); `storage.recording_key` dropped the `datetime.now(UTC)` month segment and is a pure function of (tenant, call); `_meter`'s and `_unit_price`'s docstrings were corrected against the code and against `billing.service`. `tests/pipeline_partial_failure_test.py` walks all eleven stage boundaries — kill at stage N, re-drive, compare the whole artefact census against a clean run — with an in-file negative control that doubles the lead timeline on purpose. | Measured on a real arq worker: a transient blip in `_resolve_agent`, `_upsert_call` or `mark_inbox_processed` got **1 attempt, 0 alerts, and an inbox row left `processing`** — which `claim_inbox_event` answers `duplicate` to for the whole `CLAIM_LEASE`, so the vendor's at-most-once retry was swallowed by a claim with nobody behind it and only the 10-minute poller could recover the call; that is D-31's ladder-and-guarantee inverted. The recording copy ran on every re-drive, so a repair for a MISSING EXTRACTION had to clear a vendor S3 link with no documented expiry first — an expired one failed step 1 before step 3 was reached, every hour, forever. And a second copy across a month boundary (crash between the PUT and the `recording_url` commit, or a replay of a call that ended on the 31st) landed under a second key that the retention sweep and the DPDP erasure both work from `recording_url` and cannot enumerate — a caller's voice surviving a certificate that says it was destroyed. Also pinned: a zero-length call reconstructs ₹0.20 of a ₹1.0000 charge through `SUM(qty * unit_cost_paid)` while `spend_state` takes the whole amount — `_unit_price` claimed the money never disappears, `billing.service._spend_used` always said otherwise, and the closable half is a reader in `apps/api/billing`. |
| D-149 | **The dial gate held; the batch behind it did not — a pause, a narrowed window and a withdrawn consent all leaked past the claim** | Three defects on the one path with a legal consequence, all of them in the gap between the CLAIM and the DIAL. (1) New `campaigns.service.campaign_dialable_now`, asked per contact inside the per-dial transaction, re-reads the campaign's status and its own narrowed `calling_hours` — `status = 'running'` is in the WHERE clause, so paused, cancelled, completed, deleted and another tenant's id all fail closed as one absent row. (2) `compliance.service.PERSON_LEVEL_REFUSALS` names the refusals that are facts about a PERSON (`dnc`, `no_consent`); the new `_refuse_contact` in the dispatcher reads it instead of hardcoding `"dnc"`, and is now the ONE writer of both the contact settlement and `record_compliance_block`. (3) `check_compliance_invariants.SCAN_ROOTS` gains `scripts/`, with the pilot harness's two engine reaches exempted BY NAME and re-verified every run. Tests: pause-mid-batch, window-closed-before-the-dial, withdrawn-consent-settles-and-the-campaign-completes, every-blocked-dial-is-labelled, halt-holds-while-Redis-is-unreachable, and the whole `CONSENT_STATUSES` vocabulary refusing by derivation (structural + behavioural). Runbooks `campaign-stall.md` §8 and `calls-stopped.md` corrected. | **`_dispatch_for_campaign` commits its claim and then dials one contact at a time, and `_run_tick` picks every tenant's campaigns before any of them dials — so "this campaign may dial" and "this contact is dialling" are separated by a whole dial phase, bounded only by the 300s job timeout.** The big red switch survived that gap because `check_dispatch` re-reads it per contact; the campaign's own two switches did not. A client pressing pause — or FLOWS §5's complaint-spike auto-pause, whose entire point is stopping fast — rang every contact already claimed, and a campaign narrowed to 09:00-12:00 dialled at 12:05, because `calling_hours` may only NARROW the platform window and the platform check therefore cannot see it. Separately, D-117 added `no_consent` to the gate and nothing told the dispatcher it was terminal: the contact went back to `pending` with its attempt REFUNDED every thirty minutes forever, the ladder could never exhaust it, and the surviving `pending` row meant `complete_or_rearm` never fired — so a person who withdrew permission held their campaign open, and its `campaign.completed` event, indefinitely. The metric half was a documentation lie in the opposite direction: `runbooks/campaign-stall.md` §8 sent an operator to `compliance_blocks{rule=...}` for a stalled campaign and the per-dial refusal never emitted one. And the census that claims to enumerate every way this repo can ring a phone walked `apps/` only, which is how `scripts/pilot/` — the ONE program here that dials a telephone on purpose, twice — was invisible to it; the harness is legitimate (no DB session by construction, a single `--to`, `--yes-place-real-calls-and-spend-money`, a hard `--max-calls`, and a refusal to run against production-shaped config), and now it is written down instead of unenumerated. |
| D-150 | **A figure quantized twice arrives twice — the invoice printed a line that did not multiply out — and a call finishing could un-press a client's own stop button** | `billing/service.allocate_paise` (largest-remainder apportionment) makes `_tier_totals` return ONE set of paise-exact rung minutes summing to the month's total; `usage_summary` derives `minutes_used` from them instead of running a second aggregate, so nothing downstream re-rounds a minute. `overage_rungs` is now the ONE place a rung's money is computed and BOTH the usage panel and `build_invoice` call it — which deletes `invoice._reconcile_overage` rather than keeping it. `_ZERO_PAISE` keeps a floored zero two decimals wide on the wire. `usage_summary` reads the IST billing month ONCE and hands it to `read_spend_counters` and `_spend_used`. New `caps.lock_tenant_spend_state` serializes all three writers of `spend_state.capped`, the meter's upsert included (one line in `workers/pipeline.py::_meter`). `tests/money_walk_test.py` (16) walks a rupee end to end on awkward decimals, forces the cap interleave at a barrier, and PINS two open findings; `invoice.py` records what Rule 53 and CGST s.34 do to the designed serial registry. | **The invoice was wrong by six paise on a line a client checks by hand.** 300.3s premium + 299.7s value is 5.005 + 4.995 minutes — exactly 10.00 — and `to_paise` on each rung published 5.01 + 5.00 = 10.01 against an `overage_minutes` of 10.00. `_reconcile_overage` then bent the LAST invoice line to make the total right, printing "5.00 min at ₹3.75/min ... ₹18.69". Three docstrings claimed the parts add up (`split_overage`, `tier_usage`, `invoice`) and none of them did; `tests/billing_audit_test.py` already asserted "every line multiplies out" and passed throughout, because its fixture bills whole minutes at ₹8.00 — **a rounding defect cannot show itself against round numbers.** Deriving the last part by subtraction is right for two parts and wrong for three (three buckets of 0.005 min give `total - a - b = -0.01`, a negative minute count), which is why the primitive is largest-remainder and why its "total is not the parts' total" guard is proved by a test rather than left unreachable. **The cap race reproduces on demand and a row lock cannot close it**: the meter's upsert reads the ceiling from `plans` and writes `spend_state`, so under READ COMMITTED it blocks on the row, unblocks with the new counters and the OLD ceiling (postgresql.org/docs/16/transaction-iso.html §13.2.1 — an updating command sees concurrent effects on rows it is updating, not on other rows), and overwrites the `capped` a client's stop button had just armed, mid-runaway-campaign. The module docstring's "three writers share one expression" argued definition and never inputs. **Two findings pinned, not fixed, both cross-territory and both about the same gap between quoted and charged**: a self-serve wallet is debited at `cost.total_inr` — OUR supplier cost — while the runway on the same screen prices at `self_serve_inr_per_min`, so the margin on every self-serve minute is zero and `self_serve_inr_per_min` has exactly one reader; and `usage_summary.spend_used_inr` publishes that same supplier cost to the CLIENT ("Used so far", `/c/<slug>/usage`) three paragraphs below the rule forbidding it, which also means a client's own rupee cap is denominated in our cost rather than their bill. **Also**: `tier_usage` had no caller outside tests (CLOSED by D-152), and CBIC's own pages stay unreachable from this network, so Rule 46(b)/53 and s.34 remain REPORTED, not READ. |
| D-151 | **The ledgers were immutable against the two verbs the check asked about, and erasable by the two it did not; and an exemption written for a READ was applied to every verb** | Migration `a2e9f31c605d` gives all eight `APPEND_ONLY_TABLES` a statement-level `BEFORE TRUNCATE` trigger and promotes every blocking trigger to `ENABLE ALWAYS`; `check_ledger_immutability` now verifies TRUNCATE cover and ORIGIN-vs-ALWAYS, not just UPDATE/DELETE presence.
